STATUS: IN PROGRESS — Level 1 (S01–S13) walked, read and filed; Levels 2–4 to follow.

# PyArcana learner walkthrough: S01–S52 through the QA platform

## 1. Method and persona

### Persona

**Lucía, 24, Lima.** Native Spanish speaker; reads English slowly (product names are fine,
English prose and unglossed anglicisms are friction). Finished secondary school and some
university statistics; no programming before S01. Pays for PyArcana monthly from a modest salary,
so time and money matter. Goal: a first data job (data analyst / junior data role) within a year.
Works on a mid-range Windows laptop, sometimes her phone. Takes the course in order, S01 → S52,
and knows only what earlier sections taught her.

### Method chosen, and why

The question is not "does the button work" but "would this learner understand, practise, be
assessed fairly, and come out with something she can show". No single method answers that, so
the walkthrough combines four, each for the part it is good at:

| Part of the question | Method | Source | How it was applied here |
|---|---|---|---|
| Can she do each step from what she knows? | **Cognitive walkthrough**: for each action, the four questions (will she try the right effect, notice the action, connect action to effect, see progress?) | Wharton, Rieman, Lewis & Polson, "The cognitive walkthrough method: a practitioner's guide", in Nielsen & Mack (eds.), *Usability Inspection Methods*, Wiley 1994 | Applied to every theory block, I Do demo, We Do exercise, You Do project, self-check and exam item of every section, against a fixed persona who knows only earlier sections (checked by grepping the earlier sections' text, not from memory). |
| Is the interface clear? | **Heuristic evaluation**, the ten heuristics | Nielsen, "Enhancing the explanatory power of usability heuristics", CHI '94; NN/g "10 Usability Heuristics" (2020 revision) | Used to name UI findings (H1 visibility of status, H2 match with the real world/language, H5 error prevention, H9 recovery from errors, H10 help). |
| Whose eyes? | **Persona-based usability testing** | Cooper, *The Inmates Are Running the Asylum* (1999); Pruitt & Adlin, *The Persona Lifecycle* (2006) | One fixed, written persona (above); every judgement is made as her, not as an expert. |
| Does it actually work in a browser? | **Scripted browser walk** with Playwright, reading the page as text and as the accessibility tree rather than pixels | Playwright docs: locators by role/text, `ariaSnapshot`, and the Playwright test-agent guidance (planner → generator → healer, v1.56) | `scripts/walk.mjs` takes each section as a learner does (Theory → I Do → We Do → You Do → Autocheck), runs the playground in real Pyodide, follows its hint once, answers the self-check once with one wrong answer, then clicks the result card's own button, and records progress after each step, console errors, failed requests and a phone-width overflow check. |
| Do outcomes, activities and assessment line up? | **Constructive alignment** | Biggs, "Enhancing teaching through constructive alignment", *Higher Education* 32 (1996) 347–364 | Per section: learning outcomes ↔ We Do/You Do ↔ self-check ↔ exam. Per badge: claim ↔ where taught ↔ where assessed. |
| Does each badge's evidence back its claim? | **Open Badges / credential practice**: a badge states criteria the earner can read and carries evidence that meets them | 1EdTech *Open Badges 3.0* (2024), `Achievement.criteria` and `evidence`; the catalog's own design constraints («Never exceed the evidence collected») | Section 4 table. |
| Are the exams fair measures? | **Multiple-choice item-writing guidelines** | Haladyna, Downing & Rodriguez, "A review of multiple-choice item-writing guidelines", *Applied Measurement in Education* 15(3), 2002 | Answer-position balance, longest-option cue, plausible distractors; computed over the whole bank, not sampled. |

**Why this mix and not a lab study.** A moderated test with real Peruvian beginners is the gold
standard (Nielsen's "five users" rule), and it is not possible from a sandbox. A cognitive
walkthrough is the standard substitute when users are unavailable, and its known weakness is that
it finds *learnability* problems better than *motivation* ones; the value section (5) is where that
gap is covered, explicitly as judgement.

**Who did the reading.** Each section was read in full (outcomes, theory, I Do, We Do, You Do,
self-check, the 24-item exam bank) by a reading agent working from the same written brief
(`reading/BRIEF.md`), one agent per two or three sections, in order, with the earlier sections on
hand for "taught so far" checks. The strongest claims were then re-checked by hand against the
source before they went into this report (noted where it matters). The browser walk and the QA
filing were run once per level.

**Evidence labels.** Every judgement is marked **[O]** observation (seen in the text, the browser
or the data), **[I]** inference (reasoned from what was seen), or **[G]** guess.
Severity follows the QA tutorial's own rule («¿existe una forma de seguir?», tour step 7):
**blocker** = no way on. Since the platform locks nothing (every section opens regardless of
score), a blocker here means *she cannot complete that activity from what she was taught*;
**high** = there is a way on, but it is costly or not evident (or her record/credential is wrong);
**medium**, **low**.

### What was run

| What | Result | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Existing e2e suites, `workers/billing/e2e/run.sh` (local build of HEAD `2ffbfed`, launch stage forced to beta) | ads 16/16, flows 64/64, usage 23/23 (1 SKIP: real jsDelivr unreachable), qa 15/15, cuenta 7/7, tour 4/4 | `data/baseline-e2e-run.txt` |
| `npm run test:python-content` under `.venv-content` (CPython 3.12, pinned numpy 2.2.6, pandas 3.0.5, scikit-learn 1.6.1, scipy 1.13.1), built here from PyPI | **3244 pass, 0 fail, 118 skip**, 52 sections, environment matches pins | `data/cpython/` |
| Browser walk, `scripts/walk.mjs`, Chromium, 1366×768 and 390×844, account `paid` (Pro) | per section | `data/walk/SXX.json` |
| Persona reading, `reading/BRIEF.md` | per section | `reading/SXX.md` |
| QA workspace: tutorial taken, one report per finding filed from its page, session exported | see §6 | `data/qa-tour.json`, `qa-export/` |

The CPython result matters for reading the rest: **lesson code is correct under the declared
environment**. Where something fails, it is the page, the browser runtime, the order of teaching,
or the assessment, not the Python.

## 2. Summary (interim, after Level 1)

The full summary is written when all four levels are done. After S01–S13:

- **Top blocker [O]:** passing a section's Autocheck and then clicking the result card's own
  «Marcar como completada» button *un*-completes the section, on every section, and the server
  copy loses it too (W01).
- **Exams are missing [O] and weak where they exist [O]:** a signed-in Pro learner never sees an exam
  on this build, only the self-check (W02); and in the exam bank the right answer is always «B» in
  31 of 52 sections, the longest option in almost every item (W09). Every competency badge needs
  those exams.
- **Practice is read-only [O]:** We Do exercises cannot be typed, run or checked (W05); I Do's
  «Ejecutar» shows pre-written output (W04); the one real editor per section is off-topic from S10
  on (W03).
- **Teaching order [O]:** across L1 the readings repeatedly find skills required before they are
  taught (`try/except`, loops, classes, regex syntax, SQL), and a few self-contradictions in
  graded material (S04 denominator, S03 allowlist routing, S07 email rule).

## 3. Per section

Cross-cutting findings W01–W11 are defined once in §3.0 and referenced by id in each section.

### 3.0 Cross-cutting findings (browser walk and source checks)

**W01 [high]** Al pulsar «Marcar como completada» tras aprobar el Autocheck, la sección deja de estar completada  
*Where:* first seen S01 (quiz); QA report `W01`.  
[O] After passing the self-check (≥70%), the section is already complete and the quiz sub-step is marked. The result card then offers «Marcar como completada». Clicking it calls the same toggle again (toggleSubStep/toggleSectionComplete in src/lib/progress-store.ts:54-75; onDone at SectionView.tsx:996 and the auto-call at :858-860), which UN-marks the quiz and the section. Reproduced on all 13 L1 sections (data/walk/S01..S13.json: afterAttempt1.complete=true → afterCta.complete=false). The server copy follows: GET /api/v1/me/progress shows completedSections: [] and "sec:setup": {present:false}. Severity: Alta by the QA tutorial's own rule («¿existe una forma de seguir?»): she can go on, but her record of finishing is wrong, and badges built on section completion cannot see it.  
*Expected:* Después de aprobar, «Marcar como completada» deja la sección completada (o no aparece, porque ya lo está).  
*Observed:* La sección pasa de completada a no completada; la barra «Tu progreso» no avanza; el estado del servidor también pierde la sección.  
*Fix:* src/components/course/SectionView.tsx: make onDone idempotent (set, not toggle) for quiz completion, and remove or disable the result card button once the section is complete. Add a unit test that passing then clicking keeps completion.

**W02 [high]** Con sesión Pro iniciada nunca aparece el examen: solo el Autocheck  
*Where:* first seen S01 (quiz); QA report `W02`.  
[O] Signed in as the seeded paid account (/api/v1/me → access.isPro=true, source=paid), the Autocheck tab shows the 8–12 question self-check on every section S01–S13 and never the exam. [I] Cause: the tab renders ExamView only when next-auth has a session (SectionView.tsx:318), and the static site forces next-auth session=null (src/components/Providers.tsx:10); the exam API is a Next.js route (src/app/api/exam/*) absent from the static export and from the worker. Every competency badge requires SXX-EXAM (badge_catalog.json required_activities), so on this build no learner can produce the evidence any competency badge or capstone credential needs.  
*Expected:* Un alumno Pro puede rendir el examen de la sección (3 intentos, 60 min) que el plan Pro promete («Exámenes con anti-plagio (3 variantes)»).  
*Observed:* Solo aparece el Autocheck; no hay examen ni manera de llegar a él.  
*Fix:* Serve exams from the worker (port /api/exam/start|submit|attempts to workers/billing with the D1 question bank) and render ExamView from the worker session (/api/v1/me), or stop advertising exams and exam-backed badges until then.

**W03 [high]** «Pruébalo tú mismo» no corresponde a la sección (S10: practica scikit-learn en una sección de módulos y CLI)  
*Where:* first seen S10 (theory); QA report `W03`.  
[O] The only runnable Python per section is the Theory playground (InteractivePlaygroundDemo, SectionView.tsx:1021). Its demos are keyed by section id, and the batch-A id rename (src/lib/section-id-migrations.ts) moved keys without changing content, so from S10 on most playgrounds belong to the old curriculum: S10 «Practica scikit-learn» (Pyodide downloaded scikit-learn+scipy wheels, data/walk/pyodide-requests-1-13.json), S11 «testing con asserts» in OOP, S12 «multiprocessing y logging» in APIs/SQL, S13 «tenacity y argparse» (retries everything, against S12). 38 of 43 sections from S10 to S52 are mismatched (table in REPORT.md).  
*Expected:* El editor interactivo practica lo que la sección acaba de enseñar.  
*Observed:* Practica otro tema, a veces uno que se enseña mucho después o que contradice la sección.  
*Fix:* src/components/course/SectionView.tsx InteractivePlaygroundDemo: rewrite the demos for S10–S52 from each section's own I Do (or move the map next to each section file) and add a test that each demo's title shares the section's subtopics.

**W04 [medium]** «Ejecutar y ver la salida» en Yo hago no ejecuta nada: muestra una salida escrita de antemano  
*Where:* first seen S01 (ido); QA report `W04`.  
[O] In every I Do demo (8 per section, 104 in L1) the button «Ejecutar y ver la salida» unblurs the pre-written `output` (SteppedCode.tsx:173-180). The walk saw no Pyodide traffic when pressing it (data/walk/S0x.json ido.realExecution=false). The design intent (predict, then reveal) is good, but the label says run.  
*Expected:* El botón dice lo que hace («Ver la salida»), o ejecuta de verdad.  
*Observed:* Dice «Ejecutar» y muestra texto fijo; si Lucía cambia algo en su cabeza o en su copia, la salida no cambia.  
*Fix:* src/components/course/SteppedCode.tsx: label «Ver la salida real» (and a note that it was produced with Python 3.12), or run the demo in the same Pyodide as the playground.

**W05 [high]** Hacemos juntos: 24 ejercicios sin editor, sin ejecutar y sin corrección; solo «Ver solución»  
*Where:* first seen S01 (wedo); QA report `W05`.  
[O] Every We Do exercise (24 per section, 312 in L1) shows starter code as a read-only block and a «Ver solución» button (SectionView.tsx:604-640; walk: wedo.editable=0 on all sections). There is no place to type, no run, and no check of the learner's attempt; «Verificación:» lines describe tests the learner cannot run here. For a beginner the guided→independent→transfer ladder becomes read-the-solution.  
*Expected:* Al menos los ejercicios «Independiente» y «Transferencia» se pueden escribir, ejecutar y comprobar en el navegador (el sitio ya carga Pyodide).  
*Observed:* Solo se puede leer el starter y revelar la solución.  
*Fix:* Render We Do starters in CodePlayground with the exercise's `tests` run after the learner's code (pass/fail), keeping «Ver solución» behind an attempt.

**W06 [medium]** La pista invita a cambiar el código y luego el editor dice «Output diferente al esperado. Revisa tu código.»  
*Where:* first seen S01 (theory); QA report `W06`.  
[O] S01 playground hint: «Cambia el nombre y vuelve a ejecutar». Doing exactly that (Estudiante → Estudiante 2) gives «Output diferente al esperado. Revisa tu código.» (data/walk/S01.json playground.afterEdit). The playground compares stdout to a fixed expectedOutput (CodePlayground.tsx:213-216). A minimal edit got the same red verdict in 6 of 12 L1 playgrounds that have an expected output.  
*Expected:* Experimentar no se marca como error; el mensaje distingue «salida distinta a la del ejemplo» de «tu código tiene un problema».  
*Observed:* Seguir la pista produce un mensaje de error.  
*Fix:* src/components/course/CodePlayground.tsx: after an edit, show «La salida cambió respecto al ejemplo: ¿es lo que esperabas?» instead of an error, or check properties instead of exact text.

**W07 [medium]** Los botones verdes «Completado» desmarcan el paso si se pulsan  
*Where:* first seen S01 (theory); QA report `W07`.  
[O] MarkDoneButton (SectionView.tsx:1005) shows «Completado» in green once a step is done, but it stays a button wired to the toggle. A second walk over S01 that clicked them un-marked Teoría, Yo hago, Hacemos juntos and Tú haces (S01 rerun: q1.subSteps=["quiz"]). A green «Completado» reads as a status, not as «deshacer».  
*Expected:* Un paso completado no se pierde con un clic; deshacer es explícito.  
*Observed:* Un clic en «Completado» borra el progreso del paso.  
*Fix:* SectionView.tsx MarkDoneButton: render a static status with a separate small «Desmarcar» action.

**W08 [high]** El Autocheck dice que 70% «desbloquea la siguiente sección»; no desbloquea nada y los badges exigen 85%  
*Where:* first seen S01 (quiz); QA report `W08`.  
[O] Self-check intro (SectionView.tsx:881): «Si sacas 70% o más, desbloqueas la siguiente sección». No section is locked by score (no lock code; the walk opened every section regardless). The badge catalog floors are self_check_pct 85 and section_exam_pct 85 (badge_catalog.json provisional_floors); the exam passes at 70 (src/lib/exam-scoring.ts:14). Nowhere in the learner UI is 85% mentioned (grep of src/components and src/app). Lucía passes at 75% believing she is on track for the badge.  
*Expected:* La pantalla dice qué umbral cuenta para qué: 70% para marcar la sección, 85% para los badges.  
*Observed:* Solo se menciona 70% y un desbloqueo que no existe.  
*Fix:* SectionView.tsx QuizTab intro: «Con 70% marcas la sección. Para los badges de competencia necesitas 85% en el examen.» and remove «desbloqueas».

**W09 [high]** Exámenes: en 31 de 52 secciones la respuesta correcta es siempre la opción B  
*Where:* first seen S10 (quiz); QA report `W09`.  
[O] prisma/seed.ts QUESTION_BANK: in 31 of 52 sections all 24 items have correctIndex=1 (S10–S22, S24–S30, S41–S48, S50–S52); S23 and S32 23/24. The exam start route shuffles question order but not options (src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts:215-219, options returned as stored). The correct option is also the longest in 19–24 of 24 items in every bank. S12 and S13 exams are one template («[S12/A] Sobre «requests-status-json», ¿cuál es la práctica correcta…») repeated for 24 items. A test-wise learner passes without the skill (Haladyna, Downing & Rodriguez 2002, item-writing guidelines on answer position and length cues).  
*Expected:* Posición de la respuesta balanceada/aleatoria; distractores plausibles de longitud similar; ítems que exigen la habilidad.  
*Observed:* Responder «B» o «la más larga» aprueba.  
*Fix:* Shuffle options per attempt in exam/start (store the permutation in the form) and rewrite the templated S12/S13 banks; add a seed test that fails when one position exceeds ~40% or the key is the longest option in more than half the items.

**W10 [low]** El progreso de los últimos clics no llega al servidor si se cierra la pestaña enseguida  
*Where:* first seen S13 (quiz); QA report `W10`.  
[I] Every section logged two aborted PUT /api/v1/me/progress (data/walk/S02..S13.json events). After the walk, the server copy had S13 with only theory and ido (GET /api/v1/me/progress), while the browser had theory, ido, wedo, youdo and a quiz score. [G] The sync is debounced and not flushed on pagehide/visibilitychange.  
*Expected:* Lo marcado se guarda en la cuenta aunque Lucía cierre la pestaña al terminar.  
*Observed:* Los últimos pasos se quedan solo en ese navegador.  
*Fix:* Flush pending progress with navigator.sendBeacon/fetch keepalive on pagehide.

**W11 [high]** Capstones de referencia: 66 tests son «assert True  # placeholder» y execution.json dice tests_pass: true  
*Where:* first seen S13 (youdo); QA report `W11`.  
[O] course-state/capstones/CP-N1-A…CP-N4-B/tests/test_demo.py: 66 placeholder asserts across 11 capstones (5–8 per package); only the demo-exits-0 test is real. Each execution.json records "tests_pass": true. MUST item under AGENTS.md (stand-in): it hides that no capstone behaviour is tested, and makes the gate look green.  
*Expected:* Cada prueba nombrada comprueba el caso que describe su docstring.  
*Observed:* Las pruebas pasan siempre.  
*Fix:* course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py: generate real asserts from RUBRIC.json tests_required; compute tests_pass from a real pytest run. Owner decision needed (MUST).

### Level 1 (S01–S13)

#### S01 — Entorno reproducible y trabajo seguro

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica el intérprete y tu primer script» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 8 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S01.json`.

Read: tagline, jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–6], theory[0–25] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, tests, starter, solution, output), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, retrospective, rubric), selfCheck.questions[0–7], topicEvaluations[0–3], resources; exam bank `S01-setup.exam.json` (24 items); badge catalog entries requiring S01. Checked earlier sections: none exist (S01 is first), so "taught so far" means earlier blocks of S01 itself; I grepped `S01-setup.json` for `"def `, `try:`, `assert `, `f-string`, `for `, `user.email`/`identidad`, `sys.exit`, `.split()` to find each term's first appearance.

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] Lucía is told to create a commit before the course has shown how to set the Git identity. On a fresh Git for Windows install, `git commit` stops with "Author identity unknown / Please tell me who you are". The identity step (`git config --local user.name/user.email`) appears only two blocks later, in theory[19], and in the iDo demo the commit runs with no identity step at all. On her first commit she gets an error the page has not prepared her for (walkthrough Q4 fails). — where: `theory[17].paragraphs[2]`, `iDo.steps[4].code`; identity is first taught in `theory[19].paragraphs[2]`, and the only other mention is a hint, `weDo.steps[12].hints[1]` — evidence: «Haz otra modificación pequeña, revísala, elígela con `git add` y crea un commit. El resultado correcto informa que se creó un nuevo punto del historial» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: move the identity paragraph and the `git config --local` lines ahead of theory[15] (the "qué es un repositorio" block), and add those two lines to the top of iDo.steps[4].code (the first commit must succeed on a clean machine).
- [high] [I] On Windows, the first `.venv` activation will probably fail. Windows client machines block `.ps1` scripts by default, and every activation instruction in the theory and I Do is `.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1`. The workaround (`Set-ExecutionPolicy -Scope CurrentUser RemoteSigned`, or `activate.bat`) appears only in a We Do edge case, after she has already met the red error in theory[10] and iDo.steps[2]. — where: `theory[10].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[11].paragraphs[2]`, `iDo.steps[2].code`; workaround only in `weDo.steps[6].edgeCases[0]` — evidence: «en Windows PowerShell, `.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1`» / «PowerShell: política de ejecución puede bloquear Activate.ps1 — usar Set-ExecutionPolicy -Scope CurrentUser RemoteSigned o el activate.bat» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: add a warning callout to theory[11] that quotes the Spanish PowerShell error and gives the one-line fix (Lucía's platform is the default target, not an edge case).

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The I Do tells her the commands are copy-paste ready, then gives a command line that the section itself says breaks in Windows PowerShell 5.1. `||` is a syntax error in 5.1, and bare `where` is a different PowerShell command, as theory[6] and theory[11] both warn. — where: `iDo.steps[2].code` versus `theory[6].paragraphs[3]` and `iDo.intro` — evidence: «which python || where python» versus «El PowerShell que viene con Windows 10/11 (versión 5.1) **no** admite `&&` ni `||`» and «Los bloques son comandos **copy-paste reales**» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: split the line into `which python` (Unix) and `# Windows: where.exe python` (the demo contradicts its own warning).
- [medium] [O] Many We Do starters are bash-only (`mkdir -p X && cd X`, `2>/dev/null || true`, `source .venv/...`, `GIT_PAGER=cat`), and the only Windows hint is about activation. In PowerShell 5.1 the `&&` lines fail before the exercise starts. — where: `weDo.steps[6].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[7].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[12].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[10].hints[1]`, `weDo.steps[13].hints[0]` — evidence: «mkdir -p lab_venv_t2a && cd lab_venv_t2a» / «____ 2>/dev/null || true» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: put each command on its own line, and add a PowerShell variant comment wherever an operator is shell-specific (the rubric scores "README sirve en Windows y Unix", so the exercises should model it).
- [medium] [I] In the PowerShell version of the stdout/stderr exercise, step 3 records the wrong exit code. When a command does not exist, PowerShell does not update `$LASTEXITCODE`, so the value still comes from step 2 (the missing file). Lucía will write down a "distinto de 0" code that does not belong to the command she just ran. — where: `weDo.steps[4].starterCode` (block 3), `weDo.steps[4].tests` — evidence: «comando_que_no_existe_pyarcana / echo $?  # código: ____ · mensaje: comando no encontrado» and «Bash/zsh: lee $? · PowerShell: usa $LASTEXITCODE» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: add a note that in PowerShell a missing command sets `$?` to `False` and leaves `$LASTEXITCODE` unchanged (otherwise the exercise teaches a wrong reading of the signal it is about).
- [medium] [O] The expected `git check-ignore` output does not match the solution's own `.gitignore`. In that file `.env` is on line 10 (counting comment and blank lines), not line 8. — where: `weDo.steps[21].solutionCode.output` versus `weDo.steps[21].solutionCode.code` — evidence: «.gitignore:8:.env    .env» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: change the output to `.gitignore:10:.env    .env` (Q4 fails when her correct file prints a different number from the "expected" one).
- [medium] [O] The You Do rubric gives five criteria with weights only: no levels, no descriptors, no pass threshold. Labels such as "Correctness" and "Robustness" are English and unglossed. Lucía cannot self-score, and two reviewers would score differently. — where: `youDo.rubric[0–4]` — evidence: «Robustness — README sirve en Windows y Unix (comandos de activate)» / weight «15%» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: add 0–3 descriptors per criterion, as topicEvaluations already does with `rubric_0_3`, and Spanish labels (a rubric that cannot be scored cannot gate the badge component `you_do_projects` at 80%).
- [medium] [O] The exam can be passed by picking the longest option. In 22 of 24 items the correct option is the longest, and most distractors are absurd. Two items repeat self-check questions with identical options. — where: exam items [0], [8], [17], [23] (e.g. [8] distractor «La laptop es más lenta; hay que comprar más RAM»); [6] repeats `selfCheck.questions[0]`; [12] repeats `selfCheck.questions[2]` — evidence: «Instalar pandas globalmente para forzar que Python aparezca» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (S01 exam items): rewrite the distractors as plausible misconceptions taught in the section (e.g. «`pip install` sin `-m`», «`.env` en `.gitignore` basta aunque ya esté versionado»), balance option lengths, and replace the two copied items (the exam feeds `section_exams` at 85% for `python_data_foundations`).

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The text says she has already seen `sys.exit`, but nothing before theory[7] shows it. The I Do then uses `python3 -c "import sys; print('ok'); sys.exit(0)"`. The semicolon chaining and `import` are never explained. — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «Ya viste `sys.exit` dentro de Python» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: rewrite as «Ahora verás `sys.exit(n)`: termina el programa y entrega el número n a la shell», and gloss `;` and `import sys` (false continuity makes her think she missed something).
- [medium] [O] Lucía has never programmed, and the We Do asks her to complete a script that uses an f-string, `.split()` and `[0]` indexing, none of which is explained anywhere in S01. The hint only tells her which word goes in the blank. — where: `weDo.steps[1].starterCode`, `youDo.starterCode` (`print(f"Python {sys.version.split()[0]}")`) — evidence: «version = sys.____.split()[0]» / «print(f"Hola, soy {nombre}")» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: add a one-line comment per construct (`f"…{x}"` inserts the value; `.split()[0]` takes the first word), or simplify to `print(sys.version)` (S02 teaches f-strings; S01 should not depend on them).
- [low] [O] The `||` paragraph ends with a non sequitur. It says "por eso" (that is why) you use `which`/`where.exe`, but `||` has nothing to do with locating programs. — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[3]` — evidence: «`||` es el complemento: ejecuta el segundo **si el primero falla**. Por eso en macOS/Linux se usa `which python` y en Windows `where.exe python`.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: move the `which`/`where.exe` sentence to paragraph [4], where those commands are defined.
- [low] [O] The data dictionary template describes `monthly_amount` as «Monto mensual en soles», but the sample rows include US and BR clients. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (data_dictionary template) — evidence: «C002,John Smith,US,2026-02-03,420.00» / «Monto mensual en soles» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s01-setup.ts`: either add a `currency` column or state "monto mensual (moneda: PEN para todas las filas, datos sintéticos)". This is the dictionary the capstone builds on.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [I]. Venv, freeze, `.gitignore`, `.env.example`, Ruff, branch and PR are all taught and practised in order. Two gaps can stop her on Windows: the first commit fails without a Git identity, and `Activate.ps1` is blocked by the execution policy. The `hello_env.py` starter uses an f-string and `.split()[0]` without explanation (copyable, but not understood).
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. All 8 questions map to taught blocks (venv, `.gitignore`, Conventional Commits, `install -r`, `.env`, exit code, Ruff config, force-push). They test recall only: none tests cwd vs PATH, `sys.executable` diagnosis, or `git check-ignore`, which the section treats as core.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`python_data_foundations`, plus progress badges `progress_phase0_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations`): partly. The bank covers the reproducibility critical competency (`reproducibility_determinism`: venv, pip, freeze) only as concept recall. The claim «demostró independientemente … mediante tarea práctica, pruebas ocultas» cannot be supported by MCQs where the longest option is correct in 22/24 items. The newbie description promises she "can set up a Python project from scratch", which only the You Do evidences.
- Value: fulfilled. This is practical, honest material (freeze limits, `noreply` email, force-push, secret rotation) that a junior data analyst really needs. The Windows friction (activation, `&&`/`||`, identity) is the main cost. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good. It requires a public repo with README, `requirements.txt`, Ruff config, a synthetic CSV with data dictionary, ≥3 conventional commits and an open PR. It lacks a real test (`hello_env.py` only prints) and a CI badge/demo, which is acceptable for week 1.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for Python output. All demos are `environment: local-python`, and the Python starters (`hello_sys.py`, `hello_lint.py`) print version or date, with no pandas-3 or numpy-2 reprs. [I] The section cannot be done on her phone or in the browser runtime at all (terminal, Git, venv), which the page should state up front.

#### S02 — Valores, tipos, operadores e I/O

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica el contrato raw/clean» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 11 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S02.json`.

Read: tagline, jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–12] (paragraphs, code, output, figures, callouts), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0–23] (all fields), youDo (context, objectives, requirements[0–7], starterCode, portfolioNote, retrospective, rubric), selfCheck.questions[0–10], topicEvaluations[0–3], resources; exam bank `S02-basics.exam.json` (24 items); badge catalog entries requiring S02. Checked earlier sections: `S01-setup.json` (grep for `"def `, `try:`, `assert `, `for `, `f-string`, `__name__ == `, which gave 0 hits for each) and S02's own `theory[]` (grep counts: `def ` 1, only in theory[1].code; `for ` 0; `try`/`except` 0; `assert` 0; `if ` 1, only in a figure caption).

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] The section says it will not teach conditionals, loops, functions or error recovery, yet the We Do and the You Do require her to write all four, plus `dict`, `assert` and the `if __name__ == "__main__"` guard. Nothing in S01 or S02 teaches these, so Lucía cannot do the "independent" and "transfer" exercises from understanding. She can only copy the I Do demo, which turns a construction task into transcription. — where: `theory[0].paragraphs[5]` and `theory[0].callout` versus `weDo.steps[4].instruction`, `weDo.steps[5]`, `weDo.steps[17]`, `weDo.steps[23]`, `youDo.requirements[4]`, `youDo.requirements[7]` — evidence: «en S02 prepararás sus piezas básicas sin adelantar condicionales, bucles, funciones ni recuperación de errores» versus «Completa el cuerpo de `safe_int` (vacío tras strip → error; `try int`; `ValueError` → mensaje)» and «Incluye una función `main()` y el guard `if __name__ == "__main__"`» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: either (a) add a short "sintaxis de apoyo" theory block that teaches `def/return`, `if/else`, `for`, `try/except ValueError`, dict literals and `assert` at reading level, and remove the «sin adelantar…» promise; or (b) re-scope weDo E2/E3 and the You Do to straight-line code (raw/clean/convert/print) and move `safe_int`/`parse_client` to S03/S05. Constructive alignment needs the activities to use only what was taught. The section's own hidden contract even admits the gap: «"if_for_as_support_syntax": True».

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The T1-B theory and the T1-B demo contradict each other. The theory says catching `ValueError` is an S09 skill and S02 only observes the failure. The demo for the same subtopic catches it with `try/except` and returns a tuple. — where: `theory[5].paragraphs[4]` versus `iDo.steps[1].code` — evidence: «En S09 aprenderás a capturar ese error y continuar; S02 se limita a distinguir una conversión válida de una inválida sin ocultar el fallo» versus «try:\n        return True, int(texto), None\n    except ValueError:» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: align the two. Either the theory introduces `try/except` minimally, or the demo stops at observing the error (Lucía cannot tell which rule is true).
- [medium] [O] The rounding figure contradicts the code and the exercise next to it. The figure says round once, at the end. The theory code, the demo and weDo E2 round the IGV and then the total (twice), and the We Do feedback says to quantize each amount. — where: `theory[9].figure.caption` versus `theory[9].code`, `weDo.steps[16].feedback` — evidence: «Redondear una sola vez, al final.» versus «Quantize en propina y en total, no solo “al final por suerte”.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: rewrite the caption to match the taught rule, «redondea cada monto que se guarda o se muestra; no redondees cálculos intermedios» (two opposite rules about money in one block).
- [medium] [O] The very first code block of the section defines a function that returns a dict, right after the callout says the section will not define functions. Its printed key `if_for_as_support_syntax` is untranslated internal jargon. — where: `theory[1].code` versus `theory[0].callout.content` — evidence: «Tampoco definiremos funciones» / «def section_contract():\n    return {» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: remove this code block, or replace it with plain assignments (it is reference-only, `optional: True`, and adds nothing for the learner).
- [medium] [O] The figure for the identity/mutability block is about `if` truthiness, which is not taught, and does not show the aliasing that the block explains. — where: `theory[7].figure` — evidence: «Un if desnudo funde ausente, cero y vacío en la misma rama.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: use a names→objects arrow diagram for `a`, `b = a`, `c = a.copy()` (the paragraph explicitly asks her to «dibuja tres flechas»).
- [medium] [O] The learning outcome on `input()` is never practised. Every demo and exercise forbids calling `input()`, and only recall questions test it. — where: `learningOutcomes[6]` versus `iDo.steps[6].preamble`, `weDo.steps[20].preamble` — evidence: «Capturar texto con `input()`, la función que lee lo escrito» versus «Ejecuta después; no llames `input()` aquí» and «**no** llames `input()`» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: add one local-python exercise that calls `edad_raw = input("Edad: ")`, prints `type(edad_raw).__name__`, then converts (outcome, activity and assessment should line up).
- [medium] [O] The S02 You Do breaks continuity with the S01 capstone skeleton. S01 told her S02–S04 would build the intake validator on `data/clients_synthetic.csv` (`client_id, full_name, country, signup_date, monthly_amount`). The S02 parser uses a different schema (`nombres, apellido_paterno, apellido_materno, contacto, direccion, edad`), never reads that CSV, and does not say where the file goes in the repo. — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.starterCode`; compare `S01 youDo.requirements[6]` and `S01 youDo.context` — evidence: S01: «S02–S04 añadirán el script de admisión sin rehacer esta base»; S02: «def parse_client(nombres: str, apellido_paterno: str, apellido_materno: str, contacto: str, direccion: str, edad…» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts` (or `s01-setup.ts`): use one capstone schema across S01/S02 and state the target path (e.g. `scripts/parse_client_intake.py` in `python-ds-journey`) (otherwise the "accumulating" capstone does not accumulate).
- [low] [O] An exam item models money as `float`, contradicting the section's central rule. — where: exam item [19] — evidence: «Con `nombre = "José"` y `monto = 150.5`, ¿cuál imprime el reporte con dos decimales?» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (S02 exam): use `monto = Decimal("150.5")` (the assessment should reinforce Decimal-for-soles).
- [medium] [O] The exam largely repeats the self-check. Items [0], [1], [2], [9], [10], [12], [15] and [18] reuse self-check questions [0], [1], [2], [3], [4], [5], [6] and [7] with the same or reordered options. In 17 of 24 items the correct option is the longest. So the exam measures whether she remembers the self-check rather than transfer. — where: exam [9] vs `selfCheck.questions[3]`; exam [10] vs `selfCheck.questions[4]` — evidence: «Tras `b = a` con `a = [1, 2]` y `b.append(3)`, ¿qué vale `a`?» (identical in both) — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (S02 exam): write new scenario items, e.g. predict output for `d = 7; d // -2`, or `Decimal("2.675").quantize(Decimal("0.01"))` with ROUND_HALF_EVEN, or `x = " 0 "; int(x.strip()) == 0` (the 85% badge floor should not be reachable by memorising the self-check).

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The We Do opens with a `for` loop, a list literal and a dict with tuple unpacking (`for k, (v, t) in campos.items()`) in its first three exercises. She has never seen any of them. The instruction says «En el `for`, completa…» as if `for` were known. — where: `weDo.steps[0].instruction`, `weDo.steps[2].starterCode` — evidence: «En el `for`, completa los dos huecos del `print`.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: either replace the loops with five explicit `print(repr(x), type(x).__name__)` lines, or gloss `for` in one sentence in theory[3] (walkthrough Q2: the action is not "available" from what she knows).
- [medium] [O] The solutions and hints use constructs that are not taught, such as the conditional expression, truthiness (`if not t:`), `!r`, `any(... for ...)`, `.get()` and slicing `[:]`. They appear first in a solution, so Lucía cannot check her own reasoning against it. — where: `weDo.steps[11].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[17].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[8].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[10].hints[0]` — evidence: «"nombres": nombres.strip() if nombres else None» / «if not t:» / «mapeo.get(orig, '???')» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: rewrite the solutions using only `==`, `.strip()`, assignment and f-strings, or add a one-line comment for each new construct.
- [low] [O] A theory prediction prompt asks her to reason about a "función de captura" and testing a parser, before functions or tests exist in the course. — where: `theory[11].paragraphs[4]` — evidence: «explica por qué convertir dentro de la función de captura dificultaría probar el parser» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: reword it as «¿por qué conviene guardar primero el texto y convertir en una línea aparte?»
- [low] [O] Some English jargon is left unglossed in learner text: "intake", "pipeline", "schema", "gate", "code review" and "suite", and the rubric labels "correctness/robustness/maintainability". "Intake" and "pipeline" are glossed once, but "schema" and "gate" never are. — where: `iDo.steps[7].preamble`, `weDo.steps[2].preamble`, `youDo.rubric` — evidence: «El gate del parser no es “imprimió algo”: son asserts sobre raw, Unicode y errores accionables. Esta demo reúne el schema del You Do» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s02-basics.ts`: gloss "schema" (estructura de campos) and "gate" (criterio de aprobación) at first use, or use the Spanish word (H2, match with the learner's language).

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: no [I]. It needs `def`/`return`, `if`, `try/except ValueError`, dict literals, `assert`, type hints (`tuple[bool, int | None, str | None]`), `from __future__ import annotations` and the `__main__` guard, and none of these is taught in S01 or S02. She can pass `_run_tests()` only by copying `iDo.steps[7].code` almost verbatim (it already handles the 4th case, `edad="  "`), which shows transcription rather than capability.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. All 11 questions target taught concepts (NoneType, `42` vs `"42"`, phone as str, alias, `is None`, `-3**2`, `Decimal("0.1")`, `input()`→str, raw/clean, `:.2f` on Decimal). It does not test the function/try material the activities depend on, which shows the misalignment from the other side.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`python_data_foundations`, critical competency `reproducibility_determinism`; also progress badges `progress_phase0_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, and `integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations`): partly. The bank covers types, conversion, naming, identity, precedence, Decimal and raw/clean at recall level. It does not touch functions, modules or "basic tests", which the badge's newbie description claims («use functions and modules… write basic tests»). There is nothing in S02's exam relevant to reproducibility, and the repetition of self-check items weakens the claim of independent demonstration.
- Value: mixed. The theory is clear and well chosen for a data-job learner: str vs int for phone numbers, Decimal for soles with IGV, raw/clean traceability, Unicode `Ñahui`. The practice then jumps two sections ahead, so a paying beginner will feel lost or will copy, and both waste her money. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is a single `.py` with inline asserts, has no README requirement in `youDo.requirements` (only in the retrospective), no sample input file, does not connect to the S01 repo/CSV, and has no pytest (so no green check on GitHub).
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none found. The demos use only the stdlib (`decimal`, built-ins). `list[str]`/`int | None` annotations are fine on Pyodide's Python 3.12. The `Decimal('150.50')` and `True/None` reprs are the same across versions. No pandas/numpy output, network or disk access. [I] `input()` is avoided in browser demos, so no stdin issue.

#### S03 — Decisiones y reglas de validación

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica decisiones y reglas» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 8 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S03.json`.

Read: learningOutcomes, theory[0..10] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts, figures), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0..7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0..23] (preamble, instruction, hints, feedback, starter, solution, output), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck.questions[0..7], resources; exam bank (24 items). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01-setup.json and S02-basics.json (all string fields) for `for `, `def `, `return`, `len(`, `range(`, `bool(`, `isdigit`, `-> str`, `set(`, `.get(`, `isinstance`, `repr(`, `assert`, `__main__`, `try:`/`except`, `match`; read S02 `theory[0].paragraphs[5]`, `theory[0].callout`, `theory[1].paragraphs` for the stated scope. Badge catalog: `python_data_foundations`, `progress_phase0_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations` list S03.

**Blockers**
None found. Every exercise can be finished with the hints, and the You Do's `_run_tests` defines the contract exactly.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The section gives two different statuses for `NOT_IN_ALLOWLIST`. The decision table, the I Do and the You Do test use `review`, but two We Do solutions use `reject`. A learner who copies the We Do pattern fails the You Do test `r3`. — where: `weDo.steps[14].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[16].instruction` + `solutionCode` vs `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[5].code`, `youDo.starterCode` (`r3`) — evidence: table «| `NOT_IN_ALLOWLIST` | el catálogo no reconoce el valor | `review` |»; weDo[14] solution `return {"status": "reject", "code": "NOT_IN_ALLOWLIST"}`; weDo[16] «`OUT_OF_RANGE | NOT_IN_ALLOWLIST | BAD_TYPE`» → `reject`; You Do `assert r3["region"]["status"] == "review"` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s03-decisions-rules.ts`: in weDo[14], return `review` for an unknown document type (or rename the code to something like `DOC_TYPE_NOT_ALLOWED` and state that the policy is strict). In weDo[16], move `NOT_IN_ALLOWLIST` into the `review` case, as the table does. (The section's thesis is "tabla primero; cambiar de sintaxis no autoriza cambiar una fila". Its own exercises break that rule.)
- [medium] [O] An I Do retrospective refers to data that is not in its demo. The code uses Peruvian departments, but the retrospective asks about the synthetic `R-*` codes from theory[7]. — where: `iDo.steps[4].retrospective` vs `iDo.steps[4].code` — evidence: «Compara `("R-FUERA", 30)` con `("R-COSTA", 15)`», while the code loops over `("Tacna", 30), ("Piura", 15)` — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: change it to «Compara `("Tacna", 30)` con `("Piura", 15)`». (She is asked to predict the output of pairs she never ran.)
- [medium] [O] A self-check explanation describes options that do not exist. It refers to "Error", "inválido" and "bad", but the options are other sentences, and the explanation never says why options [1] and [2] are not actionable. — where: `selfCheck.questions[7].explanation` vs `.options[0..2]` — evidence: «“Error”, “inválido” y “bad” solo anuncian que algo falló» — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: rewrite the explanation against the real distractors: [0] gives no field or action, [1] gives no value or range, [2] gives no range. (An explanation that does not match its options gives her no help when she gets it wrong; Nielsen H9.)
- [low] [O] The age validator's handling of `bool` changes between blocks. Theory rejects `True`/`False` as `BAD_TYPE`. The I Do, weDo[9] and weDo[18] drop that guard, so `True` reaches `edad < 18` and comes out as `review`. — where: `theory[6].code` vs `iDo.steps[3].code`, `weDo.steps[9].solutionCode` — evidence: theory `if not isinstance(edad, int) or isinstance(edad, bool):` vs I Do `if not isinstance(edad, int):` — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: use the bool-aware guard in every `validate_edad`. (The You Do requires «los tipos incorrectos se rechazan sin lanzar TypeError».)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] `for` loops are never taught, yet they appear in almost every snippet and Lucía has to write them. S02 explicitly defers loops, S03 never explains `for`, and S04 is the loops section. — where: `theory[4].code` (`for v in [None, 0, -5, 150]:`), `weDo.steps[1].instruction`, `weDo.steps[18].instruction`, `weDo.steps[19].instruction`, `weDo.steps[22].instruction`; S02 `theory[0].callout.content` — evidence: S02 «Todavía no usaremos condicionales ni bucles.»; S03 «2. Recorre `["DNI", "dni", "RUC"]`.»; «for val, expected in cases: assert classify_score(val) == expected» (tuple unpacking in a hint) — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: add a short "lectura de `for`" paragraph to theory[2] or theory[3] (one value per turn, the indented body, unpacking `for a, b in pares`) and state that S04 covers it fully, or give the loops in the starters so that she only writes the branch. (Question 2 of the walkthrough fails: the action she needs was never taught.)
- [medium] [O] weDo[3] asks her to classify `[]`, `{}`, `set()` and `range(0)` by truthiness, but theory says empty collections come in S06. `range` first appears in S04. — where: `weDo.steps[3].instruction`, `weDo.steps[3].starterCode`; `theory[4].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «4. Confirma que `range(0)` es falsy y `[0]` es truthy.» vs «Las colecciones vacías también son falsy, pero estudiarás sus distintas formas en S06.» — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: reduce `vals` to the scalars taught (`None, False, 0, 0.0, "", "x", 1`), or add one sentence glossing `[]`, `{}`, `set()`, `range(0)` as "colecciones vacías". (The exercise is meant to practise falsy versus absent, not new types.)
- [medium] [O] Several constructs appear with no explanation. Type hints (`score: int -> str`) are only explained in S05. `len()` is required in weDo[14]. `.isdigit()` and dict indexing `DOC_LEN[tipo]` also go unexplained. — where: `theory[5].code`, `theory[9].code`, `weDo.steps[14].hint` — evidence: «Primero `None`, luego `tipo not in ALLOWED_DOC` y después `len(numero) != DOC_LEN[tipo]`.» — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: give each a one-line inline gloss at first use, or drop the annotations from the S03 snippets. (Unexplained term at first use; grep finds no `len(`, `isdigit` or `-> str` in S01/S02.)
- [low] [I] The You Do context can be read two ways. «Parte del parser de S02» can mean "import your S02 file" or "start from where S02 left off". The starter does not use the parser at all. — where: `youDo.context` — evidence: «Parte del parser de S02 y construye el motor en cuatro pasadas.» — fix: `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: say "Los registros ya llegan convertidos (como los produce tu parser de S02); no necesitas importarlo." (The instruction should have one reading.)
- [low] [O] Untranslated English terms: «hard-fail», «OR patterns», «guard clauses», «revisor de PRs» (PR never glossed). The exam explanation cites «allowlist O(1)» before Big-O is taught in S04. — where: `exam[13].question`, `theory[6].paragraphs[3]`, `exam[0].explanation` — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (exam text) and `s03-decisions-rules.ts`: gloss each term or replace it with Spanish. (Persona: unglossed English is friction.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [I]. Guards, None versus 0, allowlist, codes and messages are all practised, and the embedded `_run_tests` names every expected code. Two things could cost her time: the `reject`/`review` conflict for `NOT_IN_ALLOWLIST` in the We Do, and the README with invariants, which no exercise practised writing.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Questions 0–7 cover None versus falsy, the policy, `if/elif`, `or`, allowlist, `match`, guard order and messages. Nothing on invariants or tests per branch (T4), and the explanation for question 7 does not match its options.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`python_data_foundations`; the progress badges claim completion only): partly [I]. The 24 items are recognition MCQs about conditionals. Many distractors can be dismissed without knowing anything («Porque if no puede comparar enteros», «Solo si Python es 2.7», «Un CSV obligatorio de pandas»), so 8 items do not show the independent ability the badge claims. Nothing touches its critical competency `reproducibility_determinism`. The badge's 85% exam floor on 8 items allows one mistake.
- Value: fulfilled. The None-versus-0 thread and the tri-state policy with stable codes is exactly what a junior data analyst needs, and it is drilled well. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: moderate. It is one `.py` file with an embedded assert suite and a demo in `__main__`, plus the README with invariants, a decision table and a before/after claim that `portfolioNote` asks for. There is no pytest, no `data/` and no CI. That is acceptable for week 3, but the README would carry the whole impression.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): no pandas, numpy or sklearn dependence. [I] `weDo.steps[2]` (starter and solution) evaluates `True is 1`. CPython 3.8+, and therefore Pyodide's 3.12, emits `SyntaxWarning: "is" with 'int' literal. Did you mean "=="?`, which the expected output does not show. She will see a warning she cannot interpret, in the exercise that teaches `is`. Mention it in the feedback, or show `True is 1` as text instead of running it.

#### S04 — Iteración y resúmenes transaccionales

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica un resumen por lotes» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 8 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S04.json`.

Read: learningOutcomes, theory[0..10] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts, figures), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0..7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0..23] (preamble, instruction, hints, feedback, starter, solution, output), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck.questions[0..7], resources; exam bank (24 items). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S03 JSON (all string fields) for `raise`, `try:`, `lambda`, `sorted(`, `pop(`, `startswith`, `sum(1 for`, `typing`, `Any`, `from __future__`, `NotImplementedError`, `__main__`, `round(`, `5xx`, and for any prose explaining `try/except` or `__main__` (none found; S02 uses `try/except` only inside exercise hints). Compared `youDo.starterCode.validate_record` with S03 `youDo.starterCode.validate_record`. Badge catalog: `python_data_foundations` (plus the progress and capstone badges) list S04.

**Blockers**
None found.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The section's central lesson is the denominator, and the course contradicts it. Theory and the figure say to divide by rows *attempted*, not *processed*. The self-check, two exam items and the theory's own closing paragraph then use «procesados» as the right denominator. — where: `theory[0].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[2].figure.caption` vs `selfCheck.questions[2].options[2]`, `exam[23].options[3]` (correct answer), `exam[12].explanation`, `theory[10].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «Dividir entre procesadas da 0.80; entre intentadas, 0.67. La tasa mejora sola cada vez que una fila falla antes de tiempo.» vs «n_total de registros procesados (intentados)» and «Un solo pase O(n) con contadores; tasa = rejects/procesados» and «Denominador = procesados/intentados.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s04-iteration-summaries.ts` (selfCheck[2]) and `prisma/seed.ts` (exam items 12 and 23): replace «procesados» with «intentados» everywhere it names the correct denominator. (She is graded on the exact distinction the section warns about, and an exam key that rewards the wrong word makes her doubt the right model.)
- [high] [O] The You Do's embedded tests do not check the section's main invariant. Counters summing to `n_total` is never asserted, and the tasa check also passes when it returns `None` for a 3-row batch. A wrong `process_batch` (for example, one that never counts `reject`) prints `tests OK`. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`_run_tests`) vs `theory[0].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: `assert s["tasa_reject"] is None or 0 <= s["tasa_reject"] <= 1`; theory «Los contadores suman exactamente el total de filas intentadas.» — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: add `assert s["n_accept"] + s["n_reject"] + s["n_review"] == s["n_total"]`, `assert s["tasa_reject"] == s["n_reject"] / 3`, and an exact count for the fixture once the aggregate rule below is fixed. (A test that cannot fail turns an unverified claim into a green check; AGENTS.md calls this worse than no test.)
- [high] [O] The You Do asks for a «status agregado» per record but never says how field statuses combine: does any `reject` win, does `review` beat `accept`? The required shape `{status, fields}` also differs from S03's `validate_record`, which returns `{campo: {...}}` with no global status. The rubric's tri-state criterion cannot be scored the same way twice. — where: `youDo.requirements[1]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`validate_record` docstring) vs S03 `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «Cada result incluye raw intacto + status agregado + detalle de campos (accept|reject|review)»; «Reutiliza lógica tipo S03: status global + detalle por campo.» vs S03 «Devuelve {campo: {status, code, message}} para edad, region, monto_ingreso.» — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: state the rule ("si algún campo es `reject` → `reject`; si no, si alguno es `review` → `review`; si no, `accept`"), show how to wrap the S03 function, and pin it in `_run_tests` with the fixture's expected counts. (Ambiguous instruction; the rubric cannot be scored.)
- [low] [O] A theory snippet demonstrates the empty-batch `None` guard and then calls `round(tasa, 4)` on the result unconditionally. With an empty list it raises `TypeError`, the very failure the paragraph warns about. — where: `theory[6].code` — evidence: `tasa = None` … `print("total", n_total, "reject", n_reject, "tasa", round(tasa, 4))` — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: print `tasa if tasa is None else round(tasa, 4)`. (She will copy this into the You Do.)
- [low] [O] Mixed advice about `zip(strict=True)`. Theory says to use it on the course's Python 3.12. An exercise says not to use it "if your environment is not 3.10+", and the I Do uses a hand-written helper instead. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[1]` vs `weDo.steps[5].preamble`, `iDo.steps[1].code`, `exam[5].question` («Python 3.9 o con helper») — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts` and `prisma/seed.ts`: drop the 3.9 hedge, and present the helper as "what `strict=True` does inside". (One rule is easier to remember.)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] She has to write `raise ValueError` and two `try/except` blocks in a transfer exercise. Exceptions are deferred to S09, and no earlier theory explains `try/except` (S02 only uses it in hints). — where: `weDo.steps[5].instruction`, `weDo.steps[5].hints[1]` — evidence: «1. Completa `zip_strict`: si longitudes difieren, `raise ValueError`.»; «Necesitas dos bloques try/except: uno que imprima DESALINEADO y otro OK.» — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: give the `try/except` scaffold in the starter so that she only writes the `if len(a) != len(b): raise ValueError(...)` line, with a two-line gloss of `raise`. (The exercise cannot be answered from what has been taught.)
- [medium] [O] Constructs beyond the theory are used or required. Generator expressions (`sum(1 for s in sts if ...)`, and a nested one in the I Do) are never explained. A set comprehension with `sorted` and a dict comprehension with `.items()` are required, although theory says other comprehensions come in S06. `.pop(0)` and `.startswith` also go unexplained. — where: `iDo.steps[7].code`, `weDo.steps[13].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[16].instruction`, `weDo.steps[17].instruction`, `weDo.steps[8].hint`; `theory[8].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: `steps_quad = sum(1 for _ in range(n) for __ in range(n))`; «Usa `{r["status"] for r in rows}` y envuélvelo en `sorted(...)`.» vs «En S06 estudiarás otras estructuras y sus comprensiones.» — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: add a short theory note ("`(expr for x in xs)` sin corchetes = la misma comprensión sin construir la lista; `{...}` crea un set, `{k: v ...}` un dict") or move weDo[16]/[17] to S06. (Unexplained term at first use.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do starter is written in syntax she has never seen: `from __future__ import annotations`, `typing.Any`, `dict[str, Any]`, `-> None`, `raise NotImplementedError`. She has to read past four unexplained lines before getting to the task. — where: `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: `from __future__ import annotations` / `def validate_record(record: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:` — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: drop the annotations (S05 teaches hints) and replace `raise NotImplementedError` with a `# TODO` that returns a placeholder, or add a "cómo leer este starter" comment. (Nielsen H2/H10: match with what she knows.)
- [low] [O] The I Do framing contradicts S03. S03 taught `def`/`return` (`theory[3]`) and its You Do required writing functions, yet S04 tells her to read `def` as an unexplained recipe. S04's own You Do then asks for three functions. — where: `iDo.intro` — evidence: «Si aparece `def nombre(...)`, léelo por ahora como una receta con nombre.» — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: "`def` funciona como en S03; en S05 estudiarás parámetros y contratos." (Avoids making her think she missed something.)
- [low] [O] Unglossed English and domain jargon: a You Do title in English, HTTP «5xx» codes, «worker», «buffer», «batch», «gate», «dashboard». — where: `youDo.title`, `weDo.steps[10].title`, `weDo.steps[8].preamble`, `iDo.steps[2].description` — evidence: «Client Intake & Data Quality Script (cierre CP-N1-A)»; «Cortar el lote en error fatal (5xx)» — fix: `s04-iteration-summaries.ts`: use a Spanish title and gloss 5xx as "código de error del servidor (500–599)". (Persona: English prose is friction.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [I]. The loop, counters, the `None` tasa and keeping `raw` are well practised. The unstated aggregation rule, the change in `validate_record`'s shape from S03, and the untaught typing syntax in the starter will cost her time, and she cannot tell whether her guess is "right".
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. It covers `range`, `zip`, the denominator, `continue`/`break`, O(n²), `while` and `enumerate`. Nothing on comprehensions (T3-B) or state tracing (T4-A), and question 2 blurs procesados/intentados.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`python_data_foundations`; the progress badges claim completion only): partly [I]. Items 15–17 do test comprehensions, which the badge's «list comprehensions instead of for-loops» claims. The answer key for item 23 contradicts the section, distractors are trivially dismissed («Siempre; for está deprecado», «Porque range es aleatorio»), and nothing addresses `reproducibility_determinism`.
- Value: mixed. The denominator thread is excellent and directly relevant to analyst work, but the core rule is undermined by the assessment wording, and the You Do's tests can pass while the summary is wrong. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak-to-moderate. One script with a 4-assert self-test and a stdout report. The README with a worked batch table is asked for (`portfolioNote`), but the tests are too weak to show to a reviewer as evidence.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none found. No pandas or numpy reprs, network or files; `zip(strict=True)` is fine on Pyodide's Python 3.12.

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica funciones con contrato» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S04 JSON (all string fields) for `lambda` (first use: S05), `try:`/`except` prose (none; S02 uses it only in hints, S04 only in code/hints), `raise`, generator expressions (`for c in raw` inside `join`/`sum`, never explained), `isdigit` (code only in S03, never glossed), `Callable`, `AssertionError`, `str | None`. None found.

- [medium] [O] The badge claims more than its required sections teach. `python_data_foundations` promises the learner can "use functions and modules to organize code" and "push your code to Git". Its required sections stop at S05, but modules are taught in S10 (`s10-modules-packaging-cli`). — where: `badge_catalog.json` → `python_data_foundations.newbie_friendly_description`, `.required_sections` — evidence: «use functions and modules to organize code» with `required_sections` `["S01","S02","S03","S04","S05"]` — fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: drop "and modules" from the description, or add S10 to `required_sections`. (A public claim must match the evidence collected.)
- [low] [O] Two exam items name demos that do not exist in the section. The closure demo is `make_norm` (I Do) or `make_phone_prefix` (We Do), and the parse exercise is `parse_monto`. — where: `exam[19].question`, `exam[10].question` — evidence: «en el sentido del demo `make_phone_normalizer(prefix)`»; «Un “error de dominio” en parse_edad("abc") se modela en el demo del curso como…» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: use `make_phone_prefix` and `parse_monto("x")`. (She will search for a demo that is not there.)
- [low] [O] An exam key rests on syntax the theory explicitly defers. `str | None` is never shown; theory postpones types that combine several types. — where: `exam[11].options[2]` vs `theory[6].paragraphs[4]` — evidence: «Porque el hint mentiría; usa Optional[str] / str | None» vs «Las formas que combinan varios tipos o varios valores de resultado se estudiarán después de presentar sus contenedores.» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: keep `Optional[str]` only, which `theory[5].paragraphs[2]` does teach. (The exam should not test deferred material.)
- [medium] [I] Many exam distractors can be dismissed without understanding, and several are unglossed jargon: «Que la función es async», «Segfault del SO», «Abre un socket por campo», «Hereda de una clase ABC obligatoria», «Una clase abstracta con MRO», «Un hilo del sistema operativo». Most items have one plausible option and three absurd ones, so the 85% badge floor measures reading, not skill. — where: `exam[5].options[2]`, `exam[10].options[2]`, `exam[12].options[1..3]`, `exam[19].options[1..2]` — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: replace them with misconceptions this section actually targets (e.g. "`f(f(x)) == f(x)` prueba que la política es correcta", "un hint `-> int` convierte el valor", "`global` es la forma segura de configurar"). (Constructive alignment: distractors should diagnose the misconceptions the section names.)
