- [high] [O] She must write `try/except` in an independent exercise and a transfer exercise, while the theory says `try/except` is taught in S09. No earlier section explains it in prose. — where: `weDo.steps[10].instruction`, `weDo.steps[11].instruction` vs `theory[4].paragraphs[4]` — evidence: «2. `try/except` para parse a int.»; «3. En el loop, captura `ValueError` y marca SKIP.» vs «S09 enseñará a continuar con `try/except`.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s05-functions-contracts.ts`: add a 4-line "lectura mínima de `try/except`" paragraph to theory[6], or give the `try/except` frame in the weDo[10] starter (weDo[11]'s starter could then keep it). (The exercise cannot be answered from what was taught; walkthrough question 2.)
- [medium] [O] `lambda` is used in an instruction and a solution without ever being explained in S01–S05. — where: `weDo.steps[17].instruction`, `weDo.steps[17].solutionCode` — evidence: «3. Demuestra default (dígitos) y lambda upper+strip.»; `norm=lambda s: s.strip().upper()` — fix: `s05-functions-contracts.ts`: use a named `def upper_strip(s): ...`, which matches the section's own advice in `theory[8].callout` («Usa un `def` con nombre para que esa responsabilidad sea visible»), or gloss `lambda` in one line. (Unexplained term at first use.)
- [medium] [O] The phone normalizer at the heart of the You Do is written as a generator expression with `isdigit`. S04 taught only bracketed list comprehensions, and `.isdigit()` is never glossed. — where: `iDo.steps[5].code`, `weDo.steps[15].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[5].hints[1]` — evidence: `return "".join(c for c in raw if c.isdigit())`; «Ramifica con if digits_only: filtra isdigit» — fix: `s05-functions-contracts.ts`: add one sentence at the first use in iDo[3]: "`c.isdigit()` es True si el carácter es un dígito; `"".join(c for c in raw if ...)` es la comprensión de S04 sin corchetes, unida en un texto." (She can copy the line but not explain it, and explaining it is what the retrospectives ask for.)
- [low] [O] Unglossed English jargon in the feedback and retrospectives: «fakes», «monkeypatch», «caller», «call site», «P1 de producción», «typecheckers», «triage del ETL», «PR». — where: `weDo.steps[17].feedback`, `iDo.steps[1].retrospective`, `weDo.steps[7].feedback`, `weDo.steps[9].feedback` — evidence: «Inyectar la fn permite tests con fakes sin monkeypatch»; «es P1 de producción» — fix: `s05-functions-contracts.ts`: use «quien llama», «doble de prueba», «prioridad máxima», «solicitud de cambio (PR)». (Persona reads English slowly; Nielsen H2.)
- [low] [O] The You Do's test harness uses constructs that are never taught: `try/except/else`, `raise AssertionError`, `Callable[[str], str]`. She does not need to edit them, but the retrospective asks her to explain which assert proves what. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`is_idempotent`, `_run_tests`) — evidence: `else:\n        raise AssertionError("normalize_email debe rechazar entradas sin @")` — fix: `s05-functions-contracts.ts`: add a comment above the `try` block: "# si NO lanza ValueError, la prueba falla a propósito". (Help/documentation; H10.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O]. All four normalizers and the orchestrator appear almost verbatim in theory[2], theory[7], weDo[1], weDo[12–16] and weDo[22]. `_run_tests` fixes the exact dict keys (`nombres`, `email`, `telefono`, `direccion`) and expected values.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. Questions 0–7 cover implicit `None`, mutable defaults, purity, LEGB, idempotence, docstring versus comment, keyword-only and the thin orchestrator; tuples and domain-result tuples (theory[5], T2-B) are missing.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`python_data_foundations`; the progress badges claim completion only): partly [I]. Functions, contracts, purity and determinism (the nearest thing to the critical competency `reproducibility_determinism`) are covered. The badge's "modules" claim has no S01–S05 evidence, two items reference non-existent demos, and the distractors are too weak to tell understanding from elimination.
- Value: mixed. The concepts (purity, idempotence, I/O at the edge, refactor behind examples) are the right ones for data work and are explained honestly, including their limits. But the You Do is mostly a transcription of We Do code, so it shows little independent work. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak-to-moderate. One file with an assert suite in `__main__`. The README with policies and limits is only suggested in `portfolioNote`, and there is no pytest, no sample data and no demo beyond one printed dict.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none found. Stdlib only (`typing`); no pandas, numpy or sklearn output, no network and no files.

#### S06 — Colecciones y estructuras de datos

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica colecciones y conflictos» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 9 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S06.json`.

Read: learningOutcomes, theory[0–8] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts), iDo.intro + steps[0–7] (preamble, code, output, why, retrospective), weDo.intro + steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, starter, solution, output, feedback, retrospective), youDo (all fields), selfCheck.questions[0–8], resources, the S06 exam bank (24 items), and the badge catalog entries that require S06. Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S05 JSON for `lambda`, `*args`/`*keys`, `json.dumps`/`import json`, `KeyError`, `AttributeError`, `try:`/`except`, comprehensions, `sorted(`, `key=`, `O(n)`/`O(1)`, `falsy`, `hashable`, `.copy()`, `isinstance`, `Callable`, `__future__`, `assert`, `pytest`.

**Blockers**
None found. Every We Do runs in the browser on built-in types, and the You Do needs only a local Python, which S01 set up.

**Issues**
- [medium] [O] The exam rewards the exact falsy-default pattern the section tells her not to use. Exam item 14 marks `for c in client.get("contactos") or []` as correct. The section says `bool(txs)` "mezcla ambos problemas" (theory[5].paragraphs[2]) and "No uses una prueba de falsedad como definición universal de ausencia" (theory[6].callout). The item also uses the key `contactos`, while the whole section uses `contacts`. — where: `S06 exam[14]` vs `theory[6].callout.content`, `weDo.steps[14].preamble` — evidence: «for c in client.get("contactos") or []» vs «no uses `bool(txs)` (castiga la lista vacía legítima)» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: make the keyed option `client.get("contacts", [])` and make `... or []` a distractor whose explanation cites the falsy rule (so the exam assesses the outcome the section taught, not its opposite).
- [medium] [O] JSON serialization is assessed but never taught in theory. No theory block mentions `json`. Yet `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False)` appears in iDo.steps[7], is required in weDo.steps[22], is one of the You Do functions (`export_deterministic`, 20% of the rubric under "Determinismo de salida") and is tested in selfCheck.questions[4]. `ensure_ascii` is explained only in a hint. The section itself says JSON belongs to S08 (theory[0].paragraphs[3] «En S08 conectarás este modelo con CSV y JSON»). — where: `theory[8]`, `iDo.steps[7]`, `weDo.steps[22].hints[1]`, `youDo.objectives[4]` — evidence: «ensure_ascii=False para tildes si hubiera.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: add a short paragraph and snippet to theory[8] introducing `import json`, `json.dumps`, `sort_keys` and `ensure_ascii` (graded work should not rest on an untaught API).
- [medium] [O] The You Do demo line is mislabelled, so a correct `get_nested` looks wrong. `main()` prints «phone C002» but calls `get_nested(store[1], "contacts", default=[])`, which returns the contacts list `[]`, not a phone. Lucía will think her helper is broken. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (main) — evidence: «print("phone C002", get_nested(store[1], "contacts", default=[]))» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: either label it `contacts C002` or give C002 a `profile` and call `get_nested(store[1], "profile", "phone", default="MISSING")` (feedback must match the action).
- [medium] [O] The rubric gives weights with no performance levels or pass conditions, so it cannot be scored consistently. For example, «Elección de estructuras justificada — 15%» does not say where or how she should justify (README? comments?). The portfolioNote asks for «dos dumps iguales obtenidos desde órdenes de entrada distintos», but that is not a requirement. The retrospective asks for asserts, but tests are not required either. — where: `youDo.rubric[0–4]`, `youDo.requirements` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: give each criterion one observable check (for example "README lists each collection with the operation it makes cheap" or "an assert shows `export(shuffled) == export(original)`") and move the two-dumps evidence into requirements (Biggs alignment: the assessment must be checkable).
- [low] [O] The theory snippet titled "unpacking" uses indexing, which weDo.steps[3] forbids. It labels `cid, region, monto = fila[0], fila[1], fila[2]` as «Unpacking fijo», while the We Do limit says «no uses índices `fila[i]` en la solución final». — where: `theory[2].code.code` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: use `cid, region, monto, canal = fila` (avoids modelling the pattern she is told to drop).

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] `lambda` is never explained, yet it is required. theory[7] deliberately uses named key functions (`region_name`, `monto_value`). From iDo.steps[6] on, every sort uses `key=lambda r: ...`. The hints give `key=lambda r: r['monto']`, and the You Do needs `key_fn=lambda r: r["id"]`. In S01–S05, `lambda` appears only once, in S05 weDo.steps[17] code, with no explanation. — where: `iDo.steps[6].code`, `weDo.steps[18–19].hints`, `youDo.starterCode` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: in theory[7], add one paragraph that rewrites `monto_value` as `lambda row: row["monto"]` and states the equivalence (first use of syntax needs a gloss).
- [medium] [O] `*path` / `*keys` in a function signature are never taught. theory[2] explains `head, *rest = fila` (unpacking). Then iDo.steps[5] (`def dig(d, *path, default=None)`), weDo.steps[15] and the You Do starter use variadic parameters, which none of S01–S05 cover (grep `*args`/`*keys`: 0 hits). Lucía may read `*keys` as the unpacking she just learned. — where: `iDo.steps[5].code`, `weDo.steps[15].starterCode`, `youDo.starterCode` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: add one sentence to iDo.steps[5].why explaining that `*path` collects extra positional arguments into a tuple and that `default=` must then be passed by name (ties back to S05's keyword-only `*`).
- [low] [O] `KeyError` and `AttributeError` are caught by name in weDo.steps[2] and weDo.steps[7], but earlier sections only showed `except ValueError`. S05 even says «S09 enseñará a continuar con `try/except`» (S05 theory[4].paragraphs[4]). The pattern is known; the names are new, and the instructions do say when each one is raised. Acceptable, but worth a one-line gloss.
- [low] [O] A self-check option lost its brackets. The question is about `d["x"]`, but the options read «get devuelve N/A; d x lanza KeyError». — where: `selfCheck.questions[7].options[1–2]` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s06-collections.ts`: restore `` `d["x"]` `` in the options (the distinction being tested is exactly that syntax).
- [low] [O] English jargon appears without a Spanish gloss: «golden files» (`weDo.steps[9].feedback`), «lookup», «shape», «snapshot», «jobs del almacén en RAM» (`weDo.steps[21].preamble`), «tradeoff» (`weDo.intro`). Each one is small, but they add friction for a slow English reader.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [I]. The four helpers mirror We Do steps 11, 13, 15 and 22. However, `export_deterministic` depends on `json.dumps`, which theory never taught, and `dedup_report(rows, key_fn)` depends on `lambda`, which nothing explains. The mislabelled «phone C002» line will make her doubt a correct solution.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. Its 9 items cover slicing, aliasing, conflicts, `.sort()` returning `None`, `sort_keys`, shallow copy, sets, `get` vs `KeyError` and flattening. That maps to 7 of the 8 outcomes. Missing-vs-empty (outcome 5) is tested only indirectly, through `get`.
- Exam tests the badge claim (independent_data_preparation, integrated_data_analyst_practice, integrated_data_science_practice, integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations; progress_phase0_walked and progress_journey_completed are participation-only): partly [I]. The exam covers duplicates, missing values and `reproducibility_determinism` (items 19–22), which serve the `data_cleaning`/`data_validation` nodes. The badge's newbie description promises pandas/NumPy and descriptive statistics, which S06 explicitly excludes (`youDo.requirements[4]` «Sin importar NumPy ni pandas»). Items 13 and 23 test recall of course structure, not skill.
- Value: fulfilled — this is a solid, honest section on choosing a data structure, and the unique + conflicts pattern is genuinely job-relevant. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is a single `memory_model.py`, with no README, tests or data file required (only suggested in portfolioNote). On her GitHub it would read as an exercise, not a project.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All snippets use built-in types plus `copy`/`json`. No pandas, numpy or file I/O; dict and set reprs are the same in 3.12.

#### S07 — Texto, Unicode y expresiones regulares

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica Unicode, regex y evidencia» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 10 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S07.json`.

Read: learningOutcomes, theory[0–10] (paragraphs, figures, code, output, callouts), iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, starter, solution, output, feedback, retrospective), youDo (all fields), selfCheck.questions[0–9], resources, the S07 exam bank (24 items), and the badge catalog entries that require S07. Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S06 JSON for `re.`, `regex`, `\d`/`\w`/`\b`, `unicode`, `casefold`, `PII`, `pytest`, `assert`, `README`. Inspected the raw starter strings with `repr()` and confirmed the cause in `src/lib/course/sections/s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`.

**Blockers**
- [blocker] [O] Three regex We Do starters ship corrupted, and the missing piece is syntax the section never teaches, so Lucía cannot reach the expected output without opening the solution. The `.ts` source writes `\w`, `\d` and `\b` with a single backslash inside a JS template literal. JS then drops the backslash (`\w` becomes `w`, `\d` becomes `d`) or turns `\b` into a backspace control character (U+0008), visible in the JSON for weDo.steps[15]. The solutions use `\\w` and are fine.
  - weDo.steps[13]: the starter pattern is `r'^(w+) (w+)$'`. It never matches «Ana Quispe», so the starter prints `None`. The feedback describes something else: «El starter invertía `group(1)`/`group(2)`». Fixing only the crossed groups, as instructed, still prints `None`.
  - weDo.steps[16]: the instruction says the only defect is case: «(E2: corrige el case del starter sin tutorial.)». After changing `[a-z]` to `[A-Z]`, the pattern `r'[A-Z]{3}-d{2}'` still returns `[]`, because `d` is a literal letter.
  - weDo.steps[15]: the instruction describes the starter as «compila `\b\d{9}\b`», but the editor shows `r'<BS>d{9}<BS>'`, with invisible control characters.

  — where: `weDo.steps[13].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[15].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[16].starterCode` (source lines ~1267, ~1349–1351, ~1393 of the `.ts`) — evidence: «pat = re.compile(r'^(w+) (w+)$')», «codes = re.findall(r'[a-z]{3}-d{2}', log)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: double the backslashes in these starters (`\\w`, `\\d`, `\\b`), and add a check that every starter `code` string is free of U+0008 and of regex escapes stripped of their backslash (the defect is invisible to CPython tests because only solutions are executed).

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The email-normalization rule contradicts itself across theory, We Do, exam and You Do. Theory says the local part keeps its case and only the domain is casefolded, and its code does `local + "@" + domain.casefold()`, giving «Ana+test@example.com». weDo.steps[9] has her casefold the whole address (`s = raw.strip().casefold()`) and expects `ok a@b.com`. The exam (item 9) keys «lower/casefold local conservador». The You Do starter comment says «strip/casefold». The You Do sample `"Ana.Perez+demo@Example.COM"` therefore has two defensible outputs, and the rubric (20%, «Email/tel con validación modesta») cannot be scored. — where: `theory[5].paragraphs[0]` + `theory[5].code`, `weDo.steps[9].solutionCode`, `youDo.starterCode` (normalize_email comment), `S07 exam[9]` — evidence: «La parte local … conserva sus mayúsculas por defecto. El dominio … se normaliza con `casefold`» vs «Usa `casefold` (no solo `lower`) por contrato» — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts` (We Do E1 solution and output, You Do comment) and `prisma/seed.ts` (exam 9): adopt the theory rule everywhere (`local + "@" + domain.casefold()`), because a single contract is the point of the section.
- [high] [O] The empty-token Jaccard contract contradicts itself. theory[8] says that if either side has no tokens there is no score: «El resultado es `None`, la razón es `sin tokens` y el caso va a `review`», and its code returns `None`. iDo.steps[6] returns `1.0` when both sides are empty and `0.0` when one is. weDo.steps[19] tells her to follow «el contrato de la demo», and the exam (item 19) keys «si ambos conjuntos están vacíos, el contrato de esta sección devuelve 1.0». Under the exam's contract, two blank names would score as an «exact» match, which the theory warns against. — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[1]` + `theory[8].code`, `iDo.steps[6].code`, `weDo.steps[19].instruction[3]`, `S07 exam[19]` — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts` (iDo.steps[6], weDo.steps[19]) and `prisma/seed.ts` (exam 19): use the theory's `None` + `review` everywhere (fail-closed matches the section's own ethic, and the exam must not key a contradicted rule).
- [medium] [O] weDo.steps[0] cannot give feedback. The starter list already contains the decomposed «José» (`'José'`, which the `.ts` converts into a real combining mark), and `repr` of the NFD form looks identical on screen. The unmodified starter therefore prints exactly the «Éxito» lines `'José'`, `'José'`, `''`. She cannot tell whether her change did anything (Q4: no visible progress). — where: `weDo.steps[0].preamble` (Éxito) + `weDo.steps[0].starterCode` — evidence: «**Éxito:** tres líneas con `repr`: `'José'`, `'José'`, `''`.» — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: make success observable, for example by also printing `len(n)` (4 vs 5 before NFC, 4 and 4 after) or `n == names[0]` (a criterion the starter already meets is not a criterion).
- [medium] [O] The rubric is weights only. «Tests de ejemplos latam — 10%» does not say whether `assert` statements in `main` count or whether a test file is expected. No testing tool has been taught yet (grep `pytest`: 0 hits in S01–S06). «Regex solo si aporta (opcional y justificada) — 10%» does not say what scores if she writes no regex at all. — where: `youDo.rubric[3]`, `youDo.rubric[5]`, `youDo.objectives[4]` — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: state the minimum ("≥3 `assert` cases covering partícula, `+tag`, `+51`; no regex scores full marks if the README says why `str` sufficed").
- [low] [O] A demo's explanation names a national-ID group that the code does not use. iDo.steps[4].why says «`m.group('dni')` en vez de índices mágicos», but the code uses `cliente_id`, and the section repeatedly forbids national IDs in practice data. — where: `iDo.steps[4].why` — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: replace with `m.group('cliente_id')`.

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] Regex syntax is never taught, yet she must write patterns. Theory and the I Do prose never explain character classes (`[0-9]`, `[A-Z]`), quantifiers (`+`, `{4}`), `\d`, `\w` or `\b` (word boundary). In theory/iDo prose, `\d` and `\w` have 0 explanations, and `\b` is mentioned only as an example of why raw strings exist. The only gloss of `[0-9]{4}` is inside a self-check explanation (`selfCheck.questions[5].explanation`). learningOutcomes[4] promises «Escribir patrones con grupos y anclas», and weDo.steps[12–16] ask her to write `[A-Z]{3}`, `\w+` and `\b9\d{8}\b`. Lucía had no programming before S01 (grep in S01–S06: no regex teaching). — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[0–2]`, `theory[7]` — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: add to theory[6] a 6-row table (`[0-9]`/`\d`, `[A-Z]`, `\w`, `+`, `{n}`, `\b`) with one example each, before the first We Do that needs them (an exercise is not answerable from untaught syntax).
- [medium] [O] Terms are used several subtopics before they are defined. «falsos negativos» appears in theory[2] (T1) but is defined in theory[9] (T4). «Jaccard» appears in theory[2].paragraphs[1] («tu Jaccard o tu exact-match fallan») but is defined in theory[8]. «tokenizar» is never glossed. «TP»/«TN» appear in theory[9] and iDo.steps[7] output, but only FP/FN are defined. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[0–1]`, `iDo.steps[7].code.output` — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: gloss at first use or move the forward references.
- [medium] [O] There is heavy unglossed English, some of it in headline positions: «overvalidation» (learningOutcomes[3], `theory[5].callout.title` «Overvalidation»), «case-insensitive», «casing» (theory[2].paragraphs[2]), «backtracking» (theory[4].paragraphs[0], used before T3-B), «plus addressing», «feature», «gate», «hang», «tunear umbrales». For a learner who reads English slowly, the learning outcome itself reads half in English. — fix: `s07-text-unicode-regex.ts`: Spanish first with the English term in parentheses («validación excesiva (*overvalidation*)»).
- [low] [O] The format spec `f"U+{ord(char):04X}"` (theory[2].code) and `{row['a']!r}` (theory[9].code) are used without explanation. They are only display helpers, but they will distract her.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [I]. Names, NFC, phone digits and `transforms` are well scaffolded by weDo.steps[0–10]. Two problems remain. The email rule she must implement is contradicted between theory and We Do, so she cannot know which output is "right". The «Tests» criterion assumes a testing practice that has not been taught. Regex is optional, which luckily spares her the untaught syntax.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. Its 10 items track the theory (NFC/NFD, a single token goes to review, `str` before regex, partículas, 0.67 goes to review, fullmatch vs search, the modest email rule, backtracking, `compile`, phone digits). FP/FN (outcome 7) is not covered.
- Exam tests the badge claim (independent_data_preparation, integrated_data_analyst_practice, integrated_data_science_practice, integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations): partly [I]. The exam fits `data_cleaning` (normalization, review over invention) and evidence-keeping well. Item 9 (email) and item 19 (empty-set Jaccard) key rules that contradict the theory. Nothing touches the badge's pandas/NumPy or descriptive-statistics claims. Item 12's distractor «Acelerar el GIL» uses an unexplained term.
- Value: mixed — the Unicode/LATAM-names half is excellent and rarely taught this well, and it fits Lucía's market exactly. The regex half shortchanges her: no syntax lesson, plus three broken starters. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good if she follows portfolioNote (README with a raw → transforms → normalized table, a review case, tests). The repo would show judgement, not just code.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. Only `unicodedata` and `re` are used. CPython 3.12 and Pyodide 0.26 (Python 3.12) share the same Unicode database, and no pandas, numpy, files or network are involved.

#### S08 — Archivos, CSV, JSON y contratos de ingesta

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica ingesta con cuarentena y manifest» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 11 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S08.json`.

Read: learningOutcomes, theory[0–11] (paragraphs, figures, code, output, callouts), iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, starter, solution, output, feedback, retrospective), youDo (all fields), selfCheck.questions[0–10], resources, the S08 exam bank (24 items), and the badge catalog entries that require S08. Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S07 JSON for `tempfile`, `io.StringIO`, `Counter`, `setdefault`, `Decimal`/`quantize` (S02: 177/29 hits), `SystemExit`/`sys.exit`, `finally`, `datetime`, `__file__`, `pytest`, `subprocess`, `venv`, `local-python`, `ausente` (S03).

**Blockers**
None found. The You Do runs in local Python, which S01 set up (`venv`: 278 mentions). All pieces are rehearsed in weDo.steps[0–23].

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The section gives two opposite "only" contracts for atomic writes. theory[3].paragraphs[1] first says «contrato único del curso, no hay otro: `tmp = path.with_name(path.name + ".tmp")`». The same paragraph then says a fixed `.tmp` name reintroduces the failure atomic writing was meant to prevent, and that the temp name must be unique per run. The theory code and callout use `tempfile.mkstemp` («Contrato del curso: temporal unico por corrida (mkstemp…)»). Meanwhile iDo.steps[1], weDo.steps[4–5], the theory[11] checklist («¿tmp es `name + ".tmp"`?»), `youDo.requirements[7]` and `selfCheck.questions[1]` all require the fixed name. Lucía is graded on the pattern the theory calls broken. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[3].code`, `theory[3].callout.content`, `theory[11].paragraphs[2]`, `iDo.steps[1]`, `weDo.steps[4].preamble`, `youDo.requirements[7]`, `selfCheck.questions[1].explanation` — evidence: «contrato único del curso, no hay otro» vs «Con un `.tmp` fijo, dos ejecuciones simultáneas … publica el contenido a medias de la otra» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s08-files-ingestion.ts`: choose one. Either teach the simple fixed `.tmp` and state its single-writer limit as a named trade-off, or make `mkstemp(dir=path.parent)` the contract in iDo, We Do, You Do, checklist and self-check (a contract that contradicts itself cannot be learned or assessed).
- [medium] [O] The rule for null email contradicts itself between theory and demo. In theory[8], `validate_schema({"id": "C1", "email": None}, ["id", "email"])` returns `(False, [], ['email'])`: null is invalid unless the field is declared `nullable`. In iDo.steps[5], the same call returns `(True, [])`, with the preamble «`email: None` **pasa** required». weDo.steps[16].feedback then says truthiness would wrongly quarantine rows «que el contrato acepta como "email desconocido"». The same goes for defaults: theory[8] says to default only when the contract declares the value («`obj.setdefault("segment", None)  # No hay un valor de negocio acordado`»), while iDo.steps[5] and weDo.steps[17] invent `'standard'`. — where: `theory[8].code`, `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[5]`, `weDo.steps[16].feedback`, `weDo.steps[17]` — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: have iDo.steps[5] and weDo.steps[17] state the contract explicitly («el contrato de esta fuente declara `segment` opcional con `'standard'` = no enviado; `email` es nullable»), so the demos follow the rule the theory sets.
- [medium] [O] The backup and hash demos contradict the theory they illustrate. theory[9] says a fixed `input.bak` overwrites the previous run's evidence, so use `backups/{run_id}/{nombre}`, and hash the backup's bytes. iDo.steps[6] writes `clients.csv.bak` and hashes `src`. weDo.steps[19] also uses `in.csv.bak`. — where: `theory[9].paragraphs[0–1]` vs `iDo.steps[6].code`, `weDo.steps[19].starterCode` — evidence: «Un `input.bak` fijo pisa la copia anterior en la segunda ejecución» — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: align the demo and exercise paths with `backups/{run_id}/…` and hash `bak.read_bytes()` (a learner copies the demo, not the paragraph).
- [medium] [O] The quarantine demo stores a reconstruction under the name the theory reserves for exact original text. theory[5].paragraphs[0] says to keep as `raw` «únicamente su texto original exacto» and to call the parsed form `parsed_row`. iDo.steps[3] stores `"raw": ",".join(row)`, which loses quoting and the original delimiter. The theory[4] callout also says «cuarentena con {raw, reason}» right after a code block that stores `parsed_row`. — where: `iDo.steps[3].code`, `theory[4].callout.content` — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: store `parsed_row` in iDo.steps[3] (or slice the original text as theory[5].code does), and correct the callout.
- [medium] [I] The self-check keys one answer where the Python docs support two. Option 1 («Para que los saltos de línea dentro de un campo entrecomillado no rompan la fila») is the first reason the official `csv` documentation gives for `newline=''`. Only option 2 is keyed. The section itself mentions quoted multi-line records (theory[5].paragraphs[0]). — where: `selfCheck.questions[10]` — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: replace option 1 with a clearly false distractor, or key "1 and 2" (an MCQ with two true options punishes the learner who read the docs).
- [medium] [O] weDo.steps[23] (transfer) is underspecified. The starter defines neither `good` nor `bad`; it calls `run` with a single broken source named `'a'`. The instruction says «Ejecuta good y bad en ese orden», and the expected output needs sources named `clients.csv,transactions.json`. She must guess the fixtures (the solution's `good` is a new one, with `n_in: 4`). The `tests` field also shows `clients.csv, transactions.json` with a space, unlike the expected output. — where: `weDo.steps[23].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[23].tests` — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: put both fixtures in the starter and make the tests field match the output exactly.
- [low] [I] Theory has a "fail-closed" demo whose expected output depends on stderr and `SystemExit`. theory[10].code ends with `raise SystemExit(main())`, where `main()` returns 1. The expected output contains the stderr line «reconcile fallo en ['clients.csv']». In a browser runner, `SystemExit(1)` may surface as a traceback or a failed run, and stdout/stderr may be shown separately. — where: `theory[10].code` — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: print `exit_code` instead of raising in the demo, or mark the block local-only.

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] Helpers used in almost every snippet are never explained. `tempfile.mkdtemp()` (theory[2], all eight iDo, most weDo) and `io.StringIO` (theory[4–5], weDo.steps[6–7]) get 0 explanations in prose. They are also absent from S01–S07 (grep `tempfile`/`StringIO`: 0 hits). Lucía will think she needs them in her ETL. — where: `theory[2].code`, `theory[4].code` — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: add one sentence in theory[2]: "`tempfile.mkdtemp()` crea una carpeta desechable para que el ejemplo no toque tus archivos; `io.StringIO` hace que un texto se comporte como un archivo abierto".
- [medium] [O] Unglossed English is dense in a section aimed at a first job: «gate» (dozens of uses, never defined), «swap atómico», «cross-device», «audit trail», «append-friendly», «streams», «fingerprint», «workspace», «handles», «exit non-zero», «Forward-compatible» (exam 16), «correlación al input» (exam 10). The jobRelevance and theory[0] are in clean Spanish, but the iDo/weDo/selfCheck layer drifts into Spanglish. — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`, `prisma/seed.ts`: use Spanish first and keep the English term only where it is industry vocabulary, glossed once.
- [low] [O] A full theory block («Crear una lista a partir de otra», theory[6], with no subtopicId) re-teaches list comprehensions. S04 already taught them (S04 learningOutcomes[5]), and S06/S07 and earlier S08 code already use them. It sits between T2-B and T3-A and breaks the T1→T4 flow. — fix: `s08-files-ingestion.ts`: delete it or move it to a pre-requisite review aside.
- [low] [O] theory[2] lists flush, fsync, directory sync and TOCTOU in one paragraph. That is accurate and well glossed, but it is four advanced ideas in one place for a learner opening files for the first time. Mark it "opcional / profundización".

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes, with one caveat [I]. Each starter function maps to a We Do step (the weDo.intro bridge map is excellent), and `run` returning an int means a "test" of the exit-1 path can be a plain `assert run(...) == 1`, which uses S02/S05 skills. The caveat is that she must implement the `.tmp` contract that the theory calls unsafe, and she has to invent the CSV/JSON column contract and the transactions file format (array or JSONL is unspecified).
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. Its 11 items cover encoding, atomic write, quarantine, reconciliation, fail-closed, the input hash, JSONL, Decimal, null vs missing, compensation and `newline=''`. Item 10 is ambiguous and item 1 enforces the contradicted fixed `.tmp`.
- Exam tests the badge claim (independent_data_preparation, integrated_data_analyst_practice, integrated_data_science_practice, integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations): partly [I]. It tests `data_validation` and `reproducibility_determinism` well (reconciliation items 9–11 and 21–22, provenance items 18–20). It tests nothing about pandas/NumPy or descriptive statistics, which `independent_data_preparation`'s description promises; the section itself defers pandas («Pandas llega en el bloque intermedio», theory[0].paragraphs[5]). Exam item 16 («Ignorar keys desconocidas») sits uneasily with theory[4].code, which fails a source on `unexpected_header`. Item 23 is a recall item that can be answered by elimination.
- Value: fulfilled — this is the most job-relevant section so far. The `n_in == n_clean + n_quarantine` discipline, compensated-error detection and input hashing are exactly what a junior data role interview probes. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: strong. README, synthetic data in `data/`, outputs, manifest and a failing-reconcile case are all required or requested in portfolioNote, so the repo would read as a real small ETL.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): low. iDo is marked `local-python`. The We Do steps have no environment set; if they run in Pyodide, `tempfile`, `os.replace`, `shutil.copy2` and `hashlib` work on its in-memory filesystem. Only theory[10] (`raise SystemExit` with stderr output, see Issues) is at risk. The You Do's `Path(__file__)` is local-only, as intended.

#### S09 — Excepciones, debugging y logging seguro

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica un lote observable y sin PII» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 11 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S09.json`.

Read: jobRelevance, learningOutcomes, theory[0–9] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts, figures), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, edgeCases, tests, feedback, starter, solution, output), youDo (all fields incl. starterCode, rubric), selfCheck.questions[0–10], resources, and the 24-item exam bank; checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S08 JSON (section + exam) for `class `, `Exception)`, `pytest`, `unittest`, `test_`, own-module imports (`from x import y` other than stdlib), `stderr`, `traceback`, `frame`, `logging`, `lambda`, `.throw(`, `yield`, `partition`, `KeyboardInterrupt`, `dead-letter`, `hashlib`, `StringIO`, `TTY`; badge catalog for `required_sections` containing S09; `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` for option shuffling.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] The You Do requires a separate test file, but nothing up to S09 teaches how to import your own module from another file or how to run a test file. Lucía can write `assert` checks (S02–S05 do teach `assert`). She cannot know that `test_audit_log.py` needs `from audit_log import ...`, or which command runs it: pytest, unittest or `python test_audit_log.py`. A grep of S01–S08 finds 0 hits for `pytest` or `test_`, `unittest` appears only as a resource link in S03, and no own-module import appears at all. Module imports arrive in S10. — where: `youDo.requirements[6]` — evidence: «Al menos 3 tests en test_audit_log.py (máscaras, fail-fast config, reconcile)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: either let the tests live in the same file as `def test_…()` functions called from `if __name__ == "__main__"` (the pattern S04 and S11 use), or add one I Do step that shows `from audit_log import mask_email` plus the exact command to run (so the 15% "≥3 tests" rubric line can be met from what was taught).

**Issues**
- [medium] [O] Two passages about traceback order contradict each other. theory[0] says to read the traceback from the bottom up, with the error and its line at the bottom. theory[4] then says frames run "from most recent to deepest (or the reverse)". In Python the most recent call *is* the deepest, so that phrase means nothing, and "o viceversa" removes the one rule she just learned. weDo[6] adds a third framing, «de afuera hacia adentro». — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[0].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «Un **traceback** lista frames del más reciente al más profundo (o viceversa según la herramienta).» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: replace it with «En Python la traza se imprime de la llamada más externa (arriba) a la más interna (abajo, *most recent call last*); la última línea es el error» (one rule, matching theory[0] and weDo[6].edgeCases[0]).
- [medium] [O] An instruction sends her to the solution, which is hidden while she attempts the exercise. — where: `weDo.steps[2].instruction` — evidence: «Paso 4: Usa un reader que lance `OSError` (como en la solución).» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: write the reader into the instruction, e.g. «define `def bad_reader(): raise OSError("no such file: data/clientes.csv")`» (H10: the step must be doable without the answer).
- [medium] [I] The You Do's central output, `errors_by_class`, is never specified. It is unclear whether it holds counts or lists, and what produces a `provider` error in a batch of plain dicts. The starter delegates classification to "type or message", and the demo data cannot produce a provider failure. The 25% rubric line «taxonomía … verificable» therefore cannot be scored the same way twice. — where: `youDo.requirements[1]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`classify_error`) — evidence: «# TODO: data | config | provider según tipo/mensaje» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: state the shape (e.g. `{"data": 2, "config": 0, "provider": 0}`), give the mapping (`TimeoutError`/`ConnectionError`→provider, `ValueError`/`KeyError`→data, `RuntimeError` from config→config), and add one demo record whose fake fetch raises `TimeoutError` (so the rubric is checkable).
- [medium] [O] The self-check tests reasons the section never gave. theory[3] says not to use a bare `except:`, but S09's theory, I Do and We Do never mention `KeyboardInterrupt` or `SystemExit` (grep: 0 hits outside selfCheck). — where: `selfCheck.questions[6]` — evidence: «Traga también KeyboardInterrupt/SystemExit y esconde corrupción» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: add that sentence to `theory[3].paragraphs[1]` (assessment must follow instruction, Biggs).
- [medium] [O] The exam is answerable by test-taking strategy. In 23 of 24 items the correct option is the longest, and many distractors are absurd. — where: exam bank (`prisma/seed.ts`, `"exceptions-logging"`) items [11], [17] — evidence: «Solo un emoji en el chat», «Enviar el log por WhatsApp» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: rewrite distractors as plausible misconceptions taught against in the section, of similar length (e.g. "retry the ValueError with backoff", "log the full row at DEBUG"). As it stands a pass does not evidence the competency.
- [low] [O] An exam distractor presents retrying an `OSError` as good practice. weDo[21] taught that `PermissionError`, an `OSError`, must not be retried. — where: exam [4] option 1 vs `weDo.steps[21].preamble` — evidence: «except OSError como recuperable de I/O y reintentar con política» / «no marques PermissionError como yes» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: narrow it to «except TimeoutError/ConnectionError como transitorio y reintentar con tope».
- [low] [O] An exam answer relies on a technique the section never taught. The section taught masking (`a***@`), not hashing or tokenising identifiers. — where: exam [17] correct option — evidence: «Token/hash truncado o campo oculto=true sin valor crudo» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: change it to «Enmascarado parcial (p. ej. `***5678`) o campo omitido».

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] The section uses `class` before the course has taught classes. `class ValidationError(Exception): pass` appears from theory[2] onward and in 7 code blocks. Classes, inheritance in parentheses and `pass` are not explained in S01–S08 (grep `class [A-Z]`: first hits are in S09), and S11 is the OOP section. Lucía has to copy syntax she cannot read. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[2]`, `weDo.steps[0].instruction` Paso 2 — evidence: «Una **excepción personalizada ligera** (`class DataLoadError(Exception): ...`) nombra el borde de tu capa» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: add one sentence: «`class X(Exception): pass` crea un tipo de error nuevo que *es un* `Exception`; S11 explica las clases a fondo» (Q2: is the action available from what was taught?).
- [medium] [O] An obscure generator trick throws errors in three places. `(_ for _ in ()).throw(...)` is used inside a `lambda` to raise an exception. Generators and `yield` are never taught (0 hits in S01–S08). She will read it as magic, or think it is required. — where: `theory[9].code`, `iDo.steps[7].code`, `weDo.steps[2].starterCode` — evidence: «load_text(lambda: (_ for _ in ()).throw(FileNotFoundError("missing")))» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: replace it with a named helper (`def missing(): raise FileNotFoundError("missing")`), which is already the pattern in the weDo[2] solution.
- [low] [O] Several terms are left in unglossed English: "frame" (used throughout T2-A, 0 hits in S01–S08), "TTY", "backoff", and a callout titled in English. — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[0–1]`, `theory[4].callout.title`, `theory[9].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[3].callout.title` — evidence: «cuando tienes TTY local», «El **backoff** creciente», «No swallow» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: gloss frame («marco: una llamada de función en la traza»), TTY («una terminal interactiva») and backoff («esperar cada vez más entre reintentos»), and retitle the callout «No tragues errores» (H2: match with her language).
- [low] [O] `str.partition` is the core of every masking helper but is never explained (0 hits in S01–S08). — where: `theory[7].code` (`mask_email`) — evidence: «local, _, domain = email.partition("@")» — fix: `s09-exceptions-logging.ts`: add a line to theory[7]: «`partition("@")` parte el texto en (antes, "@", después)».

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — masking, fail-fast, quarantine, reconcile and structured logging were each practised in We Do (weDo[15], [19], [13], [16]). The separate test file with imports was not taught, and `errors_by_class`/`classify_error` is unspecified.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — 9 of 11 items map to S09 content. [6] (KeyboardInterrupt/SystemExit) was not taught in S09. [2] (stderr) relies on S01 and is only previewed here.
- Exam tests the badge claim (applied_analytical_reasoning, integrated_data_analyst_practice, integrated_data_science_practice, integrated_ml_engineering_practice, progress_phase0_walked, integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations): no for `applied_analytical_reasoning`. Its `required_activities` include `S09-EXAM` and `S09-YOUDO`, but the claim is visualisation, BI and classical ML (`skill_nodes: python_visualization, bi_tools, classical_ml, model_evaluation`), and none of that is in S09 [O]. Partly for the three integrated badges: the exam covers "recuperación de fallos" at recognition level only. Yes for progress_phase0_walked (completion only). The exam's longest-option pattern weakens all of these [O]. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S09 and S10 from `applied_analytical_reasoning.required_sections`, or replace them with the sections that teach charts, BI and sklearn.
- Value: fulfilled — PII masking, correlation IDs and fail-fast versus quarantine are concrete, job-relevant skills for a data role in Peru (banks, retail). The 9 h estimate looks tight for 8 demos plus 24 exercises plus a project. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good if completed. It asks for a README with a taxonomy table, a masked log sample and tests, on synthetic data. No run command is specified, so the README will vary.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): two snippets print the traceback's own file name, line numbers and source lines, hard-coded as `File "demo.py", line 8`. Under Pyodide the code runs as `<exec>`, and source lines may be missing from `traceback.format_exc()`, so her output will not match the shown output [I]. They are `theory[4].code` (traceback_read.py) and `iDo.steps[2].code` (keyerror_frames.py). Everything else is stdlib (`logging`, `decimal`, `hashlib.sha256`, `io`), so I see no other risk.

#### S10 — Módulos, packaging y CLI profesional

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica scikit-learn» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 6 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S10.json`.

Read: jobRelevance, learningOutcomes, theory[0–9] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts, figures), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0–23] (all fields incl. starter/solution/output), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, the full bootstrap starterCode incl. the embedded pyproject/config/core/cli/tests/README, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck.questions[0–5], resources, and the 24-item exam bank; checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S09 JSON for `pip install -e`, `pyproject`, `Scripts\activate` (S01 covers Windows venv activation), `unittest`, `pytest`, `class [A-Z]`, `self.`, `jq`, `stderr` (taught in S01); badge catalog for S10; `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` and `src/components/course/ExamView.tsx` for option shuffling; `prisma/seed.ts` line 2653 for the source of the exam bank.

**Blockers**
None found. The bootstrap starter runs end to end on her Windows laptop once she uses the venv activation S01 taught.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] Every exam answer is option B. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and the correct option is also the longest in 23 of 24. Options reach the learner in stored order: `route.ts:116` sends `JSON.parse(q.options)` unchanged, and grep finds no option shuffle in `src/components`. A learner who always picks the second option scores 100%. S11–S14 share the same pattern; S01–S09 are balanced at 6/6/6/6. — where: exam bank `"modules-packaging-cli"` in `prisma/seed.ts` — evidence: «correctIndex: 1» ×24 — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: rebalance the answer positions, and in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` shuffle the options per attempt and remap `correctIndex` on the server (otherwise the pass at 70% and the badge's 85% floor measure nothing).
- [high] [O] An exam answer key contradicts the section. The section teaches that `None` means "not passed, this layer has no opinion" and never overrides. The exam's correct answer says `None` may mean "clear/disable". Lucía, having learned the lesson, is pushed towards a distractor. — where: exam [19] correct option vs `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `weDo.steps[19].feedback` — evidence: «Missing deja el valor previo/default; None puede significar limpiar/desactivar según contrato» vs «Un `None` en env no es «apagar jobs»: es «esta capa no opina».» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: make the key «`None` en una capa = “no opina”: no pisa la capa inferior; solo un valor explícito la reemplaza».
- [high] [O] The You Do starter is the finished solution. The bootstrap writes a complete `pyproject.toml` with `[project.scripts]`, all four subcommands, config merge and validation, stderr errors with exit codes 1 and 2, the S08 ETL, unittest tests and a README. Running one script meets requirements[0–4], [6] and [7]. The rubric (25% «Paquete editable + CLI … operables», 20% «Correctitud técnica») then scores the course's code, not hers. Compare S09's starter, which says «el starter NO es la solución». — where: `youDo.starterCode` vs `youDo.requirements[0–7]`, `youDo.rubric[0–1]` — evidence: «"""bootstrap_familiarity.py — crea un paquete real, instalable y testeable.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: leave `[project.scripts]`, the `report`/`compare` subparsers, `__main__.py` and the env/file layers as TODOs with failing tests. Constructive alignment requires the assessed artifact to be her work.
- [high] [O] The starter's README makes a false claim. It promises flags > env > file > defaults, but the code passes empty dicts for both the file and env layers and never reads `FAMILIARITY_LOG_LEVEL` or a config file. Shipping it unchanged puts a false statement on her GitHub, and requirement[5] is met on paper only. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`cli.py` `main`, `README.md`) — evidence: «config = merge_config(DEFAULTS, {}, {}, {**flags, **command_cfg})» and «Precedencia: flags > entorno > archivo > defaults.» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: read `os.environ.get("FAMILIARITY_LOG_LEVEL")` and an optional JSON config file in `main`, or make that the learner's TODO (it is the section's own learningOutcomes[6]).
- [medium] [O] The portfolio note asks for a pipe the starter cannot run. `normalize` takes a positional `text` argument and has no `-`/stdin path, although theory[7] teaches `cat data.json | familiarity normalize -`. `cat` and `jq` are also Unix tools, and Lucía works on Windows. — where: `youDo.portfolioNote` vs `youDo.starterCode` (`normal.add_argument("text")`), `theory[7].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «ejemplo de pipe `... | normalize > out.json 2> log.txt`» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: add `-` stdin support as a TODO, and give a PowerShell form (`Get-Content data.json | familiarity normalize - > out.json 2> log.txt`).
- [medium] [O] The README's own run instructions fail on a fresh checkout. The bootstrap creates no `data/` folder and no CSV, so step 3 exits with `error: [Errno 2] …` (exit 1) and she does not know whether she broke something. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (embedded `README.md` step 3) — evidence: «3. familiarity ingest --input data/clients.csv --out-dir out» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: add `"data/clients.csv": "id,name,monto\nC1,Ana,10.50\nC2,Luis,abc\n"` to `FILES` (H9: a first run should succeed or say why it failed).
- [medium] [O] Two figures are attached to the wrong theory blocks. The package-layout figure («Sin src, …») sits under «Dependencias cíclicas y API pública». The precedence figure («Cada capa pisa a la anterior», «default, archivo, entorno, flag») sits under «Layout src, pyproject.toml y builds», three blocks before precedence is taught. — where: `theory[3].figure`, `theory[4].figure` — evidence: «Cada capa pisa a la anterior. Documenta el orden o nadie sabrá por qué su valor no se aplicó.» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: move `S10-package-layout` to theory[4] and `S10-config-precedence` to theory[8].
- [medium] [O] The first I Do models the anti-pattern it warns against. It says main does not run on import, then calls `main` unguarded at module level. theory[2] likewise prints at import time under a «sin side-effects» comment. Asked to predict output, she is shown that top-level calls are fine. — where: `iDo.steps[0].code`, `theory[2].code` — evidence: «# al importar no corre main; lo invocamos explícito» followed by «raise SystemExit(main(["  José Pérez "]))» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: wrap the call in `if __name__ == "__main__":`, the guard learningOutcomes[0] teaches.
- [low] [O] A requirement and the starter use different names for the same thing. — where: `youDo.requirements[1]` — evidence: «un *entry point* de `console_scripts`» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: say «la tabla `[project.scripts]` del pyproject», as theory[0] and the starter do.

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] Packaging is only simulated until the unguided You Do. weDo[6], [7] and [10] build pyproject metadata as Python dicts and lists of path strings. The first real `pip install -e .` happens alone in the You Do, and the I Do says so. The most failure-prone step on Windows (wrong venv, `py` versus `python`, a missing `[tool.setuptools.packages.find]`) has no guided rep. — where: `iDo.steps[2].retrospective` — evidence: «Esta demo lista el contrato; el install real vive en el You Do.» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: add a guided We Do whose expected output is the real terminal transcript of `python -m pip install -e .` then `familiarity --help` (Q4: she needs to see what success looks like).
- [medium] [O] The You Do's tests use material not yet taught. They are `unittest.TestCase` classes with `self.assertEqual` and `tempfile`; classes and `self` come in S11, and unittest is never taught (no S10 theory or We Do mentions it, and S01–S09 have 0 hits). She can run the tests but cannot extend them for the 15% «Pruebas o casos de borde» line. S09 asked for tests in an unspecified style and S11 uses bare `def test_…()` functions, so the course uses three test styles in three sections. — where: `youDo.requirements[7]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`tests/test_core.py`) — evidence: «class CoreTests(unittest.TestCase):» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: use plain `def test_…(): assert …` functions with a 5-line runner, or add one I Do on unittest discovery.
- [low] [O] Several terms are left in unglossed English: `jq` (6 hits, never introduced, not on Windows by default), "spawn", "harness" and "sprints". — where: `iDo.steps[5].preamble`, `theory[6].paragraphs[2]`, `weDo.steps[15].feedback`, `theory[5].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «Cuando un operador hace `… | jq`», «sin spawn de proceso», «el harness lo imprime» — fix: `s10-modules-packaging-cli.ts`: gloss each at first use, or replace them with «otro programa que lee JSON», «sin lanzar otro proceso» and «el verificador».

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes, but hollow [O]. Running the provided bootstrap completes almost every requirement. The parts that would test her (env/file precedence, stdin `-`, sample data) are neither required by the starter nor implemented in it.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O] — all 6 items (main guard, precedence order, exit 2, stderr, minor bump, `.env`) map directly to theory and We Do. Nothing covers src layout or editable install, circular imports, or `__all__`.
- Exam tests the badge claim (applied_analytical_reasoning, responsible_machine_learning_evaluation, integrated_data_analyst_practice, integrated_data_science_practice, integrated_ml_engineering_practice, progress_phase0_walked, integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations): no for `applied_analytical_reasoning` and `responsible_machine_learning_evaluation`. Both list `S10-EXAM`/`S10-YOUDO` in `required_activities` but claim charts, BI, classical and deep ML (`skill_nodes: classical_ml, model_evaluation, deep_learning`), none of which S10 contains [O]. Partly for the integrated badges («reproducibilidad»). In every case the all-B answer key makes the exam score non-evidence [O]. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S10 from those two badges' `required_sections`/`required_activities`.
- Value: mixed — the concepts are well chosen (exit codes, stdout vs stderr, precedence, secrets) and the We Do contracts are crisp. A paying beginner, though, gets a project the course wrote for her. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: it looks professional (README, tests, installable CLI), but every learner's repo would be byte-identical. As shipped it lacks sample data and a working pipe demo, and its precedence claim in the README is untrue.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): `iDo.steps[0].code` ends with a top-level `raise SystemExit(...)`, which the browser runner may report as an uncaught exception rather than a clean exit [I]. argparse usage errors in `theory[6].code`, `iDo.steps[4].code` and `weDo.steps[13]` write `usage: familiarity …` and `error: …` to stderr, and those lines are not in the shown output; if the panel merges stderr she sees extra lines [I]. The You Do (file writes, `pip install -e .`) is declared `local-python`, so it is out of browser scope.

#### S11 — OOP y modelo de dominio

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica testing con asserts» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 6 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S11.json`.

Read: jobRelevance, learningOutcomes, theory[0–9] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts, figure), iDo.intro + iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.intro + weDo.steps[0–23] (all fields incl. starter/solution/output), youDo (all fields incl. full starterCode with the `test_domain` oracle), selfCheck.questions[0–5], resources, and the 24-item exam bank; checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S10 JSON for `self.`, `class [A-Z]`, `dataclass`, `@property`, `classmethod`, `hasattr`, `decorador` (S04 defines it in one sentence), `isinstance` (taught S02/S03), `unittest`/`pytest`; counted `self` in S11 theory paragraphs (0); badge catalog for S11; exam option order via `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`.

**Blockers**
None found. Every TODO in the You Do starter was practised in a We Do with the same names (weDo[3], [4], [9], [14], [18], [19]), and the oracle `test_domain()` tells her when she is done.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] Every exam answer is option B, and the correct option is the longest in 24 of 24 items. Options are served in stored order (`route.ts:116`, no shuffle anywhere in `src/components`), so "always pick the second" or "always pick the longest" scores 100%. — where: exam bank `"oop-domain"` in `prisma/seed.ts` (line 2943) — evidence: «correctIndex: 1» ×24 — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` and `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`: rebalance the key positions, shuffle the options per attempt with the key remapped on the server, and trim the correct options to the length of the distractors.
- [medium] [I] Two starters crash with an error the lesson does not describe. They declare `emails: list = []` and `evidences: list = []` inside a `@dataclass`, and Python's `dataclasses` refuses that when the class is defined: `ValueError: mutable default <class 'list'> for field … is not allowed: use default_factory`. Nothing is ever "shared" because no instance is ever created. The instruction, the feedback and the self-check explanation all describe the plain-function default trap instead. Lucía will see a traceback the text never mentions. — where: `weDo.steps[0].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[13].starterCode`/`.instruction`, `weDo.steps[0].feedback`, `selfCheck.questions[0].explanation` — evidence: «1. El starter comparte la lista: CF2 “ve” lo de CF1.» / «Un default `[]` se evalúa una sola vez: todas las instancias comparten la misma lista.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s11-oop-domain.ts`: say «`@dataclass` rechaza `= []` al definir la clase (ValueError) precisamente para evitar la lista compartida; `field(default_factory=list)` es la forma permitida» and show that error message as expected output (Q4: feedback must match what she sees).
- [medium] [I] Two more starters fail differently from what the text says. A non-frozen `@dataclass` with the default `eq=True` sets `__hash__ = None`, so `{a, b, c}` raises `TypeError: unhashable type`. The text instead says the set "does not collapse". The real lesson (eq without frozen means unhashable) is replaced by a wrong mental model. — where: `weDo.steps[9].instruction`, `weDo.steps[10].tests` and `.retrospective` — evidence: «1. El starter compara también el nombre — por eso el set no colapsa bien.» / «Sin frozen, el set no colapsa duplicados de valor.» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: «Sin `frozen`, una dataclass con `eq` no es hasheable: el set lanza `TypeError`. `frozen=True` la hace hasheable por sus campos.»
- [medium] [O] The ethics "tests" in theory and I Do are tautological. `test_signal_bounds` builds a score of 0.7 and asserts it lies in [0, 1], on a class with no validation, so it can never fail. The `not hasattr(..., "is_fraud")` checks run against a class that never had such a method. Later the section itself calls this kind of test theatre, so it teaches the habit first and condemns it afterwards. — where: `theory[9].code`, `iDo.steps[7].code` vs `weDo.steps[21].retrospective` — evidence: «assert 0 <= ev.signal_score <= 1» on `RelationshipEvidence("E1", "E2", 0.7)` / «Teatro de tests = falsa seguridad en el gate.» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: give the demo class its `__post_init__` and test that `RelationshipEvidence("E1","E2",1.5)` raises `ValueError`. Keep the `hasattr` check but label it a contract guard, not proof of validity.
- [medium] [O] A sentence is garbled. The gloss for "hash" lands after a long aside, so it reads as if `frozen=True` were «un número fijo». — where: `theory[5].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «habilita hash seguro —con una condición que conviene conocer antes de que falle: … exclúyelos con `field(compare=False)`— (un número fijo que Python usa para ubicar el objeto en sets y diccionarios) para sets y dicts» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: put the gloss right after «hash» and move the TypeError caveat into its own sentence.
- [low] [O] A self-check distractor is true according to the theory. theory[7] says `@runtime_checkable` lets `isinstance` check a Protocol at runtime, yet option 0 («Comprobar en ejecución…») is marked wrong. — where: `selfCheck.questions[2].options[0]` vs `theory[7].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «con `@runtime_checkable` … también en `isinstance` … en runtime» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: change the distractor to «Ejecutar get/save sin implementarlos».
- [low] [O] The You Do asks for a service the starter never scaffolds or tests. — where: `youDo.requirements[8]` vs `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «Service sin side-effects de CLI» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: add a `ClientService(repo)` TODO and one oracle assertion, or drop the requirement (otherwise the rubric cannot score it).
- [low] [O] The opening example contradicts the model. The canonical `ClientRecord` allows an empty `emails` list (`default_factory=list`, and "(sin email)" is a valid state in theory[4]), but the opening claims a valid client must have an email. — where: `jobRelevance` — evidence: «como «un cliente válido tiene email y DNI»» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: use «un cliente válido tiene `client_id` y `document_id` no vacíos».

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] `self` is never explained in the course's first OOP section. Every method takes `self`, theory[2–9] never mentions it (0 occurrences in theory paragraphs), and its first appearance anywhere is in S10's You Do tests. Hand-written `__init__(self)` with `self._d = {}` appears in four We Do starters, and the `@score.setter` syntax appears only in a starter. Lucía can copy the pattern but cannot reason about it. — where: `theory[3].code` (`def validate(self)`), `weDo.steps[8].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[11]`, `[19]`, `[22]` — evidence: «def __init__(self):\n        self._score = 0.0» / «@score.setter» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: add 3–4 sentences to `theory[2]`: «`self` es la instancia sobre la que se llama el método; `__init__` la rellena al crearla; `@dataclass` escribe ese `__init__` por ti; `@prop.setter` define qué pasa al asignar».
- [medium] [O] Some glosses repeat the word they gloss, and dozens of glosses are packed into each paragraph. That makes the theory slower to read without making it clearer. — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[2].why` — evidence: «en el pipeline de matching (el flujo de procesamiento del matching)» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: gloss each term once at first use (pipeline, matching) and drop repeat glosses (H8/H2).
- [low] [O] Some syntax is used without explanation: `...` as a method body in a Protocol (iDo[5]), `# type:` comments, and `Optional`/`Dict` mixed with `list[str]` and `X | None` (theory[7], theory[8]). — where: `theory[7].code`, `iDo.steps[5].code` — evidence: «store = FakeStore()  # type: EntityStore» — fix: `s11-oop-domain.ts`: use one modern annotation style (`dict[str, dict]`, `dict | None`) and add one line saying `...` means "sin implementación: solo el contrato".
- [low] [O] Several exam options use unglossed English jargon. — where: exam [8], [18], [20] — evidence: «Es un smell», «un solo god-object», «sin seams» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: use «mala señal de diseño», «un objeto que lo hace todo», «sin puntos de inyección».

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O] — each TODO mirrors a We Do, and the oracle gives clear progress feedback (Q4). The `self` gap means she copies rather than understands. The README, service and «diagrama textual» have no scaffold.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O] — all 6 items map to theory and We Do. [0]'s explanation misdescribes dataclass behaviour, and [2] has an arguably-true distractor.
- Exam tests the badge claim (progress_phase0_walked, integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations): partly [O]. The phase-0 badge is completion-only, so yes. The capstone credential claims a defended CP-N1-C, while the exam checks OOP vocabulary at recognition level, with absurd distractors («Entrenar un modelo de deep learning», «Conectarse a internet») and an all-B key. No competency badge depends on the S11 exam itself.
- Value: fulfilled — modelling a domain with invariants, frozen identity and Protocol fakes, plus an explicit "no verdicts" ethic, is solid and teaches a mindset a junior data analyst can transfer. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a single file with a self-checking oracle and a requested ethics README. It is not packaged into S10's `familiarity_core` (theory[8] says «La CLI de S10 llama al service»), so the portfolio story stops short of integrating with the earlier package.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none — stdlib `dataclasses`, `typing.Protocol`, `decimal` and `math`. Pyodide 0.26 ships Python 3.12, so `list[str]` and `X | None` annotations and the dataclass reprs match.

**Issues**

Variants A, B and C are identical apart from the `[S12/A]` tag. The right answer is the only option that names the concept, and it is also the longest. `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` returns `options: JSON.parse(q.options)` unshuffled, so the answer is always the second option. No item asks about status→action, `?` placeholders, rollback, egress or Haversine. — where: `S12-apis-sql-geo.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «La clave de requests-status-json es aplicarlo con trazabilidad y sin sobreclaim de evidencia.» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'apis-sql-geo'`, commented `// === S12 performance V3 recovered ===`): write 8 concepts × 3 real variants from the section's own cases. **Confusions**
- [high] [I] SQL is new here (grep S01–S11 for `SELECT`, `sqlite`, `JOIN`, `fetchone`: zero hits; earlier sections only say SQL «llega en S12»), but the section never teaches its syntax. theory[6] gives one line, «CRUD = CREATE/INSERT/SELECT/UPDATE (DELETE con cuidado…)». It never explains table/row/column, `SELECT … FROM … WHERE`, `JOIN … ON`, `ORDER BY`, `COUNT(*)`, `PRIMARY KEY`, `fetchone()` vs `fetchall()`, or why `commit()` is needed. Yet weDo.steps[12–14] ask her to write `CREATE TABLE` with `NOT NULL`, `UPDATE … SET … WHERE`, `DELETE` and a filtered `JOIN … WHERE … ORDER BY`. She can copy fragments from demos and starters, but not reason about them. The CRUD expansion also mislabels the acronym (CREATE/INSERT are listed and DELETE is in brackets). — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[1]`, `weDo.steps[13].instruction`, `weDo.steps[14].instruction` — evidence: «CRUD = CREATE/INSERT/SELECT/UPDATE (DELETE con cuidado y soft-delete si hace falta auditoría)» — fix: `s12-apis-sql-geo.ts`: add a short SQL-anatomy block before T3-A. It should have one annotated query (`SELECT columnas FROM tabla WHERE condición ORDER BY …`), a 3-row table drawn as text, and a line each for `fetchone`/`fetchall`/`commit`. Write CRUD as Create/Read/Update/Delete = INSERT/SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE. (For a would-be data analyst, SQL is the core job skill. Currently she learns *safe* SQL before learning SQL itself.)
- [medium] [O] Unglossed jargon at first use: «WGS84» (theory[9].paragraphs[1], grep: no earlier hit), «DLQ» (theory[3].figure.alt), «runbook» (weDo.steps[2].preamble «una acción de runbook»; first glossed only in S13 theory[9]), «full-sync» (iDo.steps[1].why), «heap»/«socket» (glossed, but the paragraph stacks six English terms). — where: as listed — evidence: «estima km entre dos puntos WGS84 con radio R=6371 km» — fix: `s12-apis-sql-geo.ts`: gloss inline at first use («WGS84, el sistema de coordenadas del GPS»; «runbook, el manual de operación»). (Lucía reads English slowly; theory[2–9] average several anglicisms per sentence.)
- [low] [O] Every We Do starter ends with `print('ok', True)`, which prints «ok True» while the DEFECT is still present. No solution prints it, and only weDo.steps[0] and [4] hint that she should remove it («sin texto extra»). — where: `weDo.steps[0..21,23].starterCode.code` — evidence: «print('ok', True)» — fix: `s12-apis-sql-geo.ts`: delete the line from starters. (It is a false success signal, the opposite of walkthrough question 4.)
- [low] [O] The geocoder contract keys change between theory and the I Do: `{"lat","lon","label"}` in theory[5] versus `{"lat","lon","provider"}` in iDo.steps[3]. The fallback mode is called `offline_fallback` in theory/I Do and `offline` in weDo.steps[10]. — where: `theory[5].code`, `iDo.steps[3].code`, `weDo.steps[10]` — evidence: «REQUIRED = {"lat", "lon", "provider"}» — fix: `s12-apis-sql-geo.ts`: use one key set and one mode string. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — each stub mirrors a We Do she has solved (require_token, should_retry, cache, provenance, normalize, allowlist, MockGeocoder, Haversine, join), so the smoke path is reachable by copying. But the timeout, offline fallback, transactions and contract tests in the rubric have no stub, and the SQL in `build_db`/`case_join` relies on syntax that was shown but never explained.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the 7 items cover 400 vs retry, token location, f-string SQL, egress, distance ≠ kinship, pagination end and rollback count. Nothing covers contract tests/fallback, coordinate validation, JOIN or provenance fields, and no item asks her to read code.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations`, `progress_phase0_walked`; no competency badge requires S12 alone): no [O] — the bank is 24 copies of a generic «aplicar X con evidencia verificable» item that is always option 2. It cannot evidence «reproducibility_determinism» or anything else, and the 85% exam floor of the capstone credential is met by pattern-matching.
- Value: mixed — the engineering habits are strong and correctly taught (status before body, selective retry, `?` placeholders, atomic rollback, egress allowlist, distance as signal, not verdict). But a beginner aiming at data-analyst jobs pays for 9 hours here and gets almost no SQL querying (no `WHERE` explanation, no `GROUP BY`/aggregates), a garbage exam and an off-topic playground. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak as briefed. It is one `cp_n1c_acquisition.py` with a print-only smoke path, no tests, no README template beyond the three screenshots in `portfolioNote`, no data file, and a mock HTTP layer a reviewer may read as a toy. With a real `tests/` folder and a short README stating the N1 retry and egress policy, it would read as a credible junior adapter.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): [I] `sqlite3` is an unvendored stdlib module in Pyodide that must be loaded explicitly, and `CodePlayground.tsx` `REQUIRED_PACKAGES` has no `sqlite3` entry. So theory[6]/[7], iDo.steps[4]/[5], weDo.steps[12–17] and the You Do would raise `ModuleNotFoundError` if pasted into the playground. These blocks render as non-runnable `CodeBlock`s marked `local-python`, so this only bites if she uses the browser editor. No pandas-3/numpy-2 reprs or network calls (all URLs are `example.com` strings, never fetched).

Lucía, and especially Lucía on her phone, would see `| # | Condición | Estado |` / `|---|-----------|--------|` as text. — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[2..8]` — evidence: «| 2 | uncertainty = `high` (aunque el score sea 0.95) | `needs_review` |» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: write the five rules as a numbered list, or put the table in one paragraph and add GFM table support to `src/components/course/RichText.tsx`. A pair at 0.75 is `needs_review` by `decide_ops_status` but is not in `clerical_queue`, so nobody reviews it. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[1].code` (`int(score >= 0.7)`), `weDo.steps[4]`, `theory[7].paragraphs[6..8]` — evidence: «Scores en banda intermedia (p. ej. [0.4, 0.7]) **nunca** auto-mergean … Aceptar solo si score ≥ 0.8» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: make the clerical queue `review_low <= score < accept_min` (0.40/0.80) everywhere and change the iDo prediction threshold to 0.8. The reconciliation (multi-key blocking by document) appears only in prose. No demo, We Do or You Do implements a second blocking key, so she cannot see how a cross-block pair ever reaches `er_score`. — where: `theory[2].figure.caption`, `theory[2].paragraphs[1–2]` — evidence: «0.5 si solo el documento coincide (bloques distintos — sospecha de migración o error de región)» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: add `candidate_pairs(records)` to iDo.steps[0] or weDo T1-A-E3, using the union of the name|region block and the doc block, and show that the doc-only pair arrives through the second key. (Two statements she cannot both hold.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do asks for far more than the starter scaffolds, and part of the starter claims to be finished when it is not. The starter (`familiarity_dashboard.py`) contains none of: published fixtures, a `pr_metrics`, a txs function, a case-card/explain function, a tests file, or a module named `demo_n1_dashboard`, so the required command fails with «No module named demo_n1_dashboard». Its docstring says «privacy_sheet y level1_regression_notes ya están listos», but `level1_regression_notes()` returns descriptions with no pass/fail or evidence column, so it cannot meet rubric[4]. — where: `youDo.requirements[3,5]`, `youDo.rubric[0,4]`, `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «4) Corrige pseudonymize; privacy_sheet y level1_regression_notes ya están listos» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: ship the You Do as a small package (`demo_n1_dashboard.py` or `__main__`, `fixtures.json`, `tests/test_rules.py`) and give `LEVEL1_REGRESSION_MATRIX` `status`/`evidence` fields to fill in. Yet the portfolio note asks for «captura del dashboard con 3 casos» and the requirements ask for «Dashboard scaffold 3 casos + mapa/tooltips». She will not know whether a terminal screenshot counts, or whether she is expected to learn a plotting library on her own. — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[0]`, `youDo.objectives[5]`, `youDo.portfolioNote` — evidence: «tres fichas + puntos de mapa con coords sintéticas (Lima/Arequipa) y tooltips de geoseñal» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: state explicitly that the N1 "dashboard" is a text/Markdown case sheet (e.g. `render_case_sheet(cases) -> str` written to `dashboard.md`) and that the "map" is a lat/lon table. Alternatively, add a 20-line stdlib HTML export. (The section title promises a dashboard; the deliverable is undefined.)

The exam slugs are English. — where: `title`, `youDo.title`, `theory[1].paragraphs[4]` — evidence: «el paquete CF-1 —privacidad, demostración y runbook— entregado» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: translate the title («Tablero de evidencia de familiaridad», as `catalog.ts` already does), spell out CF-1 at first use, and drop «wire». (Lucía reads English slowly; this section closes the level she paid for.)
