**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — `choose_stack`, caché, `build_safe_request`, `minimize_payload` and `field_match_rate` are direct reuses of We Do and are doable. Untaught or unscaffolded: the localhost HTTP fixture, how to produce `pred` from `input`, a golden set an honest mock can match, the «baseline comparado» (no baseline appears in any demo or exercise), and the README (no template). There is no solution or expected run output to check against.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the five items cover rules vs LLM, schema_fail, injection, no auto-fraud and the circuit breaker, but all are policy recall with absurd distractors («Exportados a prensa»-style). Nothing checks `field_match_rate`, the `/1000` cost formula, `issubset` direction or licence vs `not_for`, which are the computations the We Do drills.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase1_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; `progress_journey_completed`): no [O] — the bank is answerable by position (key always 1), so it certifies nothing. The progress badge's claim («including … section exam for each section») is literally met. The capstone credential's `critical_competencies` (`reproducibility_determinism`, `communication_audience_tuned`, `business_framing_judgment`) get no S25 items. Its `newbie_friendly_description` says the N2 capstones cover «RAG, deep learning», which S14–S26 never teach (grep: 0 hits).
- Value: mixed — the governance thinking is unusually good and interview-relevant: rule vs model vs LLM, licence ≠ ethics, schema-first, injection as design, and score ≠ fraud. But for a paying beginner, nine hours of "AI endpoints" with no AI call, no prompt written and a trivial exam is thin. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one script with three `NotImplementedError`s, a golden set that rewards hard-coding, and no tests folder, fixture file, README template or real or local model. A recruiter would read "AI assistant" and find a keyword `if`.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): `youDo.starterCode` `call_local_endpoint` (`urllib.request.urlopen` to localhost) cannot work in Pyodide, which has no sockets. `theory[4]` `transformers.pipeline` is commented out, correctly. Everything else is stdlib (`hashlib`, `re`, `json`, `dict | dict`), and its outputs are version-independent.

#### S26 — Orquestación y VP RPA + AI Analyst

*Browser walk:* not run for S26.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S26-integrator-phase1.json` (learningOutcomes, jobRelevance, theory[0–9], iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.steps[0–23] incl. starter/solution/feedback, youDo, selfCheck, resources) and all 24 items of `S26-integrator-phase1.exam.json`. Also read: the S26 playground (`src/components/course/SectionView.tsx` `demos['integrator-phase1']`), `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`, badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` listing S26 (and the `required_sections` of the capstone credential's prerequisite badges), `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` (CP-N2-A/B/C, `GATE_MAP`), and every file in `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-{A,B,C}/` (BRIEF, IDO/WEDO/YOUDO, RUN, FINAL_INTERFACE, RUBRIC.json, gate.json, execution.json, evidence_manifest.json, system_or_data_card.md, demo.py, data/generate.py, tests/test_demo.py, STARTER/). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S25 `.json` for `DAG`, `topol`, `deque`, `cron` (whole word), `DLQ`, `dead-letter`, `backoff`, `idempot`, `ACID`, `SLO`, `runbook`, `on-call`, `drain`, `superseded`, `E2E`, `CF-2`, `CF-1`, `Familiarity`, `expir`, `example.test`, `redact`, `openpyxl`, `load_workbook`, `zip(`, `any(`, `all(`, `jitter`, `RAG`, `deep learning`, `sklearn`.

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] The S26 You Do and the CP-N2-C gate define different products, so Lucía can finish one and fail the other. CP-N2-C's acceptance tests require «aprobación expira → no se envía», «logs no contienen correos», «rerun del mismo job → mismo resultado» and an interface `rpa.run(job) -> RpaAudit` over an «Excel sintético de ventas mensuales». The S26 You Do has no expiry, no log redaction, no Excel ingest, no `rpa.run`, and a `ckpt` that stores step names, not job/item ids. Its stage names also differ from CP-N2-C's `demo.py` (`ingest_excel … human_approval, email_draft, completed`, with no AI step at all). «Sin idempotencia» and «Logs con PII» are P0s she is never pointed to. Expiry and recipient allowlists were taught in S22 (grep `expir`: 58 hits), but S26 never asks her to reuse them. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/BRIEF.md` («Criterios de aceptación», «Interfaz»), `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` vs `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «`rpa.run(job) -> RpaAudit` — Recibe job (dict con excel path + spec)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: make the You Do starter expose `run(job) -> dict` with `draft`, `approvals`, `audit_trail`, `rollback_available` and `logs_redacted`. Read an `.xlsx` the S20 way, and add S22's `expires_at` check and a `redact()` for logs. Or rewrite `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json,demo.py}` to the S26 7-step contract. (The gate must grade what the section built.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do starter already "passes" everything without any work, and it models the human approval as automatic. `advance("approve")` appends `{"action": "approve", "actor": "r1"}` by itself, `hitl` starts at all zeros, and `package_e2e()` hard-codes `"n2_regression": "pass"`, `cost_tokens 1200` and `value_minutes_saved_est 45`. Running the untouched starter prints seven `success`, `can_draft_after True` and `package pass 45`. iDo.steps[7] and theory[9] do the same thing with literals such as `print("audit", …, "fraud_labels", 0, "n2_regression", "pass")`, while the preamble asks her to predict «por qué `n2_regression` es `pass` (re-run real)». — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`advance`, `package_e2e`), `iDo.steps[7].code`, `theory[9].code` — evidence: «"n2_regression": "pass",  # re-run real: CP-N2-A/B/C + privacy + CF-2» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: make `approve` require an explicit `decide(action, actor, reason)` call from outside `run_all`. Compute `n2_regression` from an actual `subprocess.run` of the three capstone test files, and start `hitl` with pending items. (This is a tautological check: the section's own rule «no "planned"» is broken by its scaffold, and the "human" in HITL is the code.)
- [high] [O] The exam bank has the key at index 1 for all 24 items, and options are served unshuffled (`route.ts` l.116). The distractors are jokes («Chistes del equipo», «Únicamente el favicon», «Número de emojis en logs»). Several items also rely on concepts S26 explicitly does not model: «jitter», «thundering herd» (theory says «sin cap ni jitter»), «upstream_failed», «versionado optimista o partición», «Message-ID». — where: `S26-integrator-phase1.exam.json[0..23]`, items 2, 7, 10, 17 — evidence: «Backoff exponencial con jitter en retries busca: … evitar thundering herd» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'integrator-phase1'`): write items from the section's own cases, e.g. backoff for attempt 4 with base 100 (→800), `any` vs `all` on the queues, `put_once` result, the cycle case, reject without reason. Shuffle in `route.ts`. (The Level-2 closing exam is passable by position.)
- [medium] [O] The DAG theory claims cycle detection that no code performs. Theory says «si hay ciclo, el pipeline **no arranca**», but `topo()` (Kahn) silently returns a shorter list on a cycle. iDo.steps[0]'s `order_from_edges` only follows a linear chain and silently drops any branch. No exercise adds a cycle test, so Lucía leaves believing her topo sort protects her. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[2].code`, `iDo.steps[0].code` — evidence: «si hay ciclo, el pipeline **no arranca**» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: add `if len(order) != len(nodes): raise ValueError("ciclo")` to `topo`, plus a We Do that feeds a cycle. (A stated invariant with no check behind it.)
- [medium] [O] The rubric makes the level-promotion requirement a bonus. Theory says promotion «exige … la regresión de S14 a S26 y CF-2» and that the regression «no se "compensa"», yet the You Do rubric gives the regression/CF-2 criterion the weight «bonus checklist». CP-N2-C has a further two incompatible rubrics: `RUBRIC.json` (0–3, ≥2.4) and `gate.json.scoring_weights_reference` (tests 0.15, `pass_threshold` 0.8). — where: `youDo.rubric[6]`, `theory[1].paragraphs[3]`, `CP-N2-C/gate.json` — evidence: «"weight": "bonus checklist"» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: make regression a pass/fail gate criterion; `course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py`: emit a single rubric. (Lucía cannot tell what she will be graded on.)
- [medium] [O] The S26 playground is off-topic and contradicts the section's ethics. `demos['integrator-phase1']` scores people from `edad` and `ingreso` into «ALTO/MEDIO/BAJO» risk, with no human review. It has no `expectedOutput`, and it prints timing values that change on every run. — where: `SectionView.tsx` `demos['integrator-phase1']` — evidence: «risk = "ALTO" if score > 0.7 else "MEDIO"» over «{"edad": 0.8, "ingreso": 0.6, …}» — fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with the 7-step `run_vp` runner with `fail_at` and an `expectedOutput`. (It is an automated adverse label on age, the exact thing S25/S26 forbid.)
- [low] [O] Prose and code disagree in small ways that matter to a careful reader. theory[6] says the case is «analysis=1, report=1, recipient=0 → `blocked True`», but the code has all three pending and prints `all_clear False`. theory[7] specifies audit `{action, actor, ts, reason?}` and «Actor = id sintético (`r1`, `r2`)», but the code has no `ts` and uses actors «ana»/«luis». theory[7]'s `act()` does not enforce «reject exige reason». — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[7].code` — evidence: «act("report-1", "approve", "luis")» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: align each code block with its paragraph. (She is told to predict outputs and will predict from the prose.)
- [low] [O] The weDo.steps[23] solution makes `n2_regression` a free-text string, contradicting theory[9]'s `n2_regression=pass`. — where: `weDo.steps[23].solutionCode` — evidence: «"n2_regression": 'CP-N2-A/B/C critical+privacy'» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: use a `{"status": "pass|fail", "tests": [...]}` value. (The evidence field should be checkable.)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] «CF-2» is never defined, and it points to a concept last seen in S13. A grep of S01–S25 finds 0 hits for `CF-2`. «Familiarity» appears only in S13, and CF-1 was never expanded there either. Yet CF-2 is a promotion requirement and a You Do deliverable. — where: `theory[0].callout`, `theory[9].callout`, `youDo.objectives[4]` — evidence: «CF-2 fija interfaces entre Familiarity, reporting y automatización» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: spell out CF-2 at first use, list the exact interfaces (function signatures) she must verify, and say how. (Q2: the knowledge needed is not on the page.)
- [medium] [O] «VP» is used both for a person and for the system. S20 glossed it as «vicepresidencia o área de negocio», but here «el VP no autoetiqueta fraude», «path canónico del VP», «runbook del VP». The title «Orquestación y VP RPA + AI Analyst» is unglossed English. — where: `title`, `theory[9].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «**`fraud_labels=0`** (el VP no autoetiqueta fraude)» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: title it «Orquestación del flujo RPA + analista IA», and use «VP» only for the business owner. - [low] [O] Six We Do starters carry an English, unaccented boilerplate line. — where: `weDo.steps[4,6,8,16,17,19].starterCode` — evidence: «# Este codigo tiene un defecto intencional que el learner debe corregir.» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: remove it (the `# DEFECT:` line already says it). (Noise in learner-facing code.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — each piece (topo order, backoff, DLQ, create-once, superseded, `any` gate, audit, SLO alert) was drilled, and the starter even runs. But the We Do exercises are mostly one-token fixes on literals (e.g. `>100`→`>60`, `all`→`any`). Nothing makes her assemble resume-from-persisted-checkpoint, a real retry loop or a reject→requeue cycle, and the starter already prints success. CF-2, the N2 regression and the CP-N2-C criteria (expiry, redaction, Excel, `rpa.run`) are undefined or unscaffolded.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the 8 items cover path order, the `any` gate, `fraud_labels=0`, DLQ+owner, `run_id` immutability and compensation, all of which were taught. The distractors are mostly absurd («Borrar S14», «Exportados a prensa»), and nothing checks backoff arithmetic, idempotency or the cycle rule.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase1_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; `progress_journey_completed`): no [O] — the key is always index 1. `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent` lists `prerequisite_badges` `applied_sql_query_development` (needs S37) and `applied_rag_llm_service_development` / `responsible_machine_learning_evaluation`. Those map to S20/S21 and S10/S23 through stale slugs ("rag", "fastapi", "sklearn", "computer-vision"), which are now Excel, Documents, CLI and Playwright. The Level-2 capstone credential therefore cannot be earned at the end of Level 2, and its description promises «SQL, RAG, deep learning», which S14–S26 do not teach. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: drop S37-dependent prerequisites, re-map the stale badges and rewrite the description from the real titles.
- Value: mixed — the operational ideas (DLQ with owner, compensation as `superseded`, three-queue HITL, «si esto se cae… ¿quién se entera?») are excellent and rare for a data-analyst track. But Level 2 closes on a self-approving starter, a hard-coded "regression pass", a positional exam and a gate that grades a different product. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a single script whose output is green before she writes a line. No data, no tests, no Excel, no AI step connected to S25. As shipped it would not survive a reviewer asking "what does `pass` mean here?".
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none in lesson code (stdlib only: `collections.deque`, dicts, `zip`; no pandas or numpy reprs). The S26 playground prints `time.time()` deltas, which are nondeterministic and have no `expectedOutput`. A real Excel ingest (needed for CP-N2-C) via `openpyxl` would need `micropip` in the browser, and file paths on disk will not exist there.

### Capstone checks from the readings

**L1 capstones**
Judged as Lucía publishing each one to her GitHub profile from what `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-{A,B,C}/` ships (README, tests, data, demo).
- [high] [O] All three test suites are tautological. In each `tests/test_demo.py`, 5 of 6 tests are `assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check`. The sixth only checks that `demo.py` exits 0 and prints `METRICS_JSON:`. Each `execution.json` nonetheless records «"tests_pass": true». CP-N1-C's `demo.py` also contains `assert … or len(review_queue) >= 0` (always true) and hard-codes `"status": "pass"` in its metrics, which is the «flag de aprobado» S13 forbids. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-{A,B,C}/tests/test_demo.py`, `execution.json`, `CP-N1-C/demo.py` — evidence: «assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check» — fix: `course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py`: generate one real assert per `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` entry, set `tests_pass` only from a real run, and remove the tautological assert and the hard-coded status. (A hiring reviewer who opens `tests/` sees placeholders, which is worse than no tests. AGENTS.md calls tautological tests worse than none.)
- [high] [O] CP-N1-C's reference `demo.py` contradicts S13 on every contract. Its ER is `0.7*name-Jaccard + 0.3*phone`, where S13 uses doc+block 1.0/0.5/0.0. Its relationship score is `0.6*geo + 0.4*phone`, where S13 uses 0.5/0.3/0.2. Its thresholds are 0.75/0.35 rather than 0.80/0.40, and it outputs the decision label «auto_same_entity_candidate». The phone feeds both scores, which is the mixing S13 warns against. `a.get("phone_last4") == b.get("phone_last4")` scores two missing phones as shared, which is the `''==''` bug weDo.steps[6] teaches her to fix. The demo has no privacy sheet and no correction mechanism, its own P0s. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-C/demo.py` — evidence: «rec["decision"] = "auto_same_entity_candidate"» — fix: `CP-N1-C/demo.py`: re-implement it from the S13 You Do solution. (A learner who compares her work with the reference will "fix" correct code toward the wrong one.)
- [medium] [O] The data are placeholders, and the same generator ships with all three capstones. `data/generate.py` is byte-identical across A, B and C except for one comment. It returns three generic records («Ana Sintetico», «Bruno Demo», an empty name), defines an unused `rand`, and is never read by `demo.py`. CP-N1-B's BRIEF promises «clients.csv … y transactions.json», and CP-N1-C's promises «entity_id, signal_type, signal_value, provenance, confidence, timestamp». — where: `CP-N1-{A,B,C}/data/generate.py` — evidence: «{"id":"SYN-003","name":"","email":"bad","amount":50,"status":"failure"}» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py`: emit capstone-specific fixtures that match each BRIEF's «Datos», and have `demo.py` load them. (A data-job portfolio is judged first on its data.)
- [medium] [O] The STARTER is empty and the README is three lines long. Every `STARTER/cp_n1_*.py` is `raise NotImplementedError`, and every `STARTER/README.md` says «Copia este directorio y completa `cp_n1_c.py` según `BRIEF.md`». For CP-N1-C there are now three competing entry points (`STARTER/cp_n1_c.py`, S13's `familiarity_dashboard.py`, and the required `python -m demo_n1_dashboard`), and none of them is `familiarity.review` from `FINAL_INTERFACE.md`. All the BRIEFs list wrong prerequisite sections (CP-N1-A «S03 (estructuras de datos)», CP-N1-B «S06 (NumPy) … S08 (pandas)»). `catalog.ts` assigns `badgeId 'capstone_foundations'`, which does not exist in `badge_catalog.json`. — where: `CP-N1-*/STARTER/*`, `CP-N1-*/BRIEF.md`, `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` l.59–70 — evidence: «S05 (OOP), S06 (NumPy), S07 (adquisición de datos), S08 (pandas)» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py` + `catalog.ts`: generate BRIEFs from the live section titles, name a single entry module per capstone matching its interface, use `badgeId: 'integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations'`, and give each capstone a README template (problem, how to run, sample output, tests, limits, privacy). (Lucía cannot tell which file is "the" deliverable.)
- Verdict: as briefed, none of CP-N1-A/B/C would read well on a GitHub profile. A reviewer would see stdlib scripts that print JSON, identical toy data, placeholder tests, an empty starter and English/Spanish naming drift. The ideas underneath, though, are portfolio-grade and unusually mature for Level 1: A's validation with correct denominators, B's quarantine plus an idempotent manifest hash, and C's ER≠REL with abstention and a privacy sheet. Once they have real tests, real fixtures, a README with run instructions and sample output, and one visible artifact each (a summary table for A, a manifest for B, a Markdown case sheet for C), they would be a credible trio for a junior data role.

Every `STARTER/cp_n2_*.py` is `raise NotImplementedError`, and every `STARTER/README.md` says only «Copia este directorio y completa…». The BRIEFs list «S15 (stdlib deep), S16 (wxPython GUI), S17 (packaging)» (A), «S20 (RAG…), S21 (FastAPI)» (B) and «S23 (computer vision) … S25 (streamlit)» (C). The real sections are Pandas, Calidad, Joins, Excel, Documentos, Playwright and Endpoints IA. `catalog.ts` assigns `badgeId 'capstone_independent'`, which does not exist in `badge_catalog.json`. — where: `CP-N2-*/STARTER/*`, `CP-N2-*/BRIEF.md` «Prerrequisitos», `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` l.71–82 — evidence: «S22 (rapidfuzz/entity), S23 (computer vision), S24 (RPA advanced), S25 (streamlit)» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py` + `catalog.ts`: generate the BRIEFs from the live section titles, use `badgeId: 'integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent'`, and ship a README template (problem, how to run, sample output, tests, limits, privacy). (Lucía would go back to «S16 wxPython» for help and find data cleaning.)
- Verdict: as shipped, none of CP-N2-A/B/C would read well on a GitHub profile. A reviewer would see stdlib scripts printing JSON with `"status": "pass"` hard-coded, identical toy data, placeholder tests, an empty starter and no visual output, from a level whose sections taught pandas, matplotlib, openpyxl and Playwright. The briefs themselves are portfolio-grade and well chosen for a junior analyst: A is EDA with a 5-category memo, B is an accessible report with source→claim traceability, and C is Excel→report→approval→draft with rollback. With real fixtures, real tests per `tests_required`, a rendered artifact each (an HTML/PDF EDA report, a chart-checked dashboard, a draft `.eml` plus an audit JSON) and a README with run instructions, they would make a credible trio.
